Non-abelian descent types
Nguy e ^ ~ \tilde{\hat{\text{e}}} n M a ⋅ \d{\text{a}} nh Linh

Abstract
We present the notion of non-abelian descent type, which classifies torsors up to twisting by a Galois cocycle. This relies on the previous construction of kernels and non-abelian Galois 2-cohomology due to Springer and Borovoi. The necessity of descent types arises in the context of the descent theory where no torsors are given a priori , for example, when we wish to study the arithmetic properties such as the Brauer–Manin obstruction to the Hasse principle on homogeneous spaces without rational points. This new definition also unifies the types by Colliot-Thélène–Sansuc, the extended types by Harari–Skorobogatov, and the finite descent type by Harpaz–Wittenberg.



Keywords.
Galois cohomology, torsors, kernels, non-abelian cohomology.

1
Introduction

1.1
Context of the problem

Let
X
be a (smooth and geometrically integral) variety defined over a number field
k
. A fundamental question in arithmetic geometry is to ask whether
X(k)\neq\varnothing
. An obvious necessary condition is that the set
X(k_{\Omega}):=\prod_{v\in\Omega}X(k_{v})
is non-empty, where
\Omega
denotes the set of places of
k
. If this condition is also sufficient (that is, if either
X(k_{\Omega})=\varnothing
or
X(k)\neq\varnothing
), we say that
X
satisfies the
Hasse principle
. This is the case, for example, when
X
is a projective quadric hypersurface (Hasse–Minkowski) or a principal homogeneous space of a simply connected semisimple linear algebraic group (Kneser–Harder–Chernousov). In general, the Hasse principle does not always hold; counterexamples exist already within the class of projective cubic surface or that of principal homogeneous spaces of a torus.

In 1971, Manin
[
Man71
]
introduced a pairing between
X(k_{\Omega})
and the Brauer–Grothendieck group
\operatorname{Br}(X):=\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{G}_{m})
. By the global reciprocity law (Albert–Brauer–Hasse–Noether), this pairing cuts out a subset
X(k_{\Omega})^{\operatorname{Br}}
of
X(k_{\Omega})
containing (the diagonal image) of
X(k)
. The emptiness of
X(k_{\Omega})^{\operatorname{Br}}
is often referred as the
Brauer–Manin obstruction to the Hasse principle
. It is natural to ask if this obstruction is the only one (
i.e.
, if the non-vacuity of the Brauer–Manin set
X(k_{\Omega})^{\operatorname{Br}}
is sufficient for the existence of
k
-rational points on
X
). This is still too much to expect. Indeed, Skorobogatov constructed a bielliptic surface which is a counterexample to the Hasse principle not explained by the Brauer–Manin obstruction
[
Sko99
]
.

A conjecture of Colliot-Thélène predicts that the Brauer–Manin obstruction is the only for smooth proper
rationally connected
(a notion due to Campana, Kollár, Miyaoka, and Mori) varieties (see
[
CT03
]
). Examples of rationally connected varieties are (geometrically) unirational varieties such as homogeneous spaces of connected linear algebraic groups. A method to attack Colliot-Thélène conjecture is the
descent theory
. Its idea is to reduce an arithmetic problem (such as the Brauer–Manin obstruction) on
X
to the same problem on its
descent varieties
. More precisely, let
f:Y\to X
be a
torsor
under a linear algebraic group
G
, that is,
Y
is equipped with a right action
Y\times_{k}G\to Y
of
G
such that the morphism
f
is fppf and
G
acts simply transitively on its fibres. For each Galois cocycle
\sigma:\operatorname{Gal}(\bar{k}/k)\to G(\bar{k})
, we may perform an operation called
twisting
to obtain a torsor
f^{\sigma}:Y^{\sigma}\to X
under an inner
k
-form
G^{\sigma}
of
G
(see paragraph
1.2
below). The set of
k
-rational points of
X
has the following partition (see
e.g.

[
Sko01
, p. 22]
):

X(k)=\bigsqcup_{[\sigma]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)}f^{\sigma}(Y^{\sigma}(k)),

where the notation
[\sigma]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)
means
[\sigma]
is a cohomology class represented by a Galois cocycle
\sigma:\operatorname{Gal}(\bar{k}/k)\to G(\bar{k})
. If we could show a similar identity for the Brauer–Manin sets, namely

X(k_{\Omega})^{\operatorname{Br}}=\overline{\bigcup_{[\sigma]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)}f^{\sigma}(Y^{\sigma}(k_{\Omega})^{\operatorname{Br}})},

in the topological space
X(k_{\Omega})
(equipped with the product of
v
-adic topologies), then the Brauer–Manin obstruction to the Hasse principle is the only obstruction for
X
, as soon as it is the case for the twisted torsors
Y^{\sigma}
. The problem with this approach is that sometimes we need descent theory when no torsors are given
a priori
. In other words, the
existence
of torsors satisfying certain conditions has to be taken into account. For example, if
X
is a homogeneous space of a connected linear algebraic group
G^{\prime}
, then we do not have a torsor
G^{\prime}\to X
unless
X
already has a
k
-rational point! Worse, the stabilizer
\bar{G}
of a geometric point
x\in X(\bar{k})
needs not be defined over
k
.

The above difficulty has motivated the author to define a notion weaker than that of torsor, the
descent type
, which is the main subject of study in this article.
Grosso modo
, a descent type
\lambda
on
X
consists of a Galois equivariant torsor
f:\bar{Y}\to X\times_{k}\bar{k}
under an algebraic
\bar{k}
-group
\bar{G}
and a class
\eta
in a non-abelian 2-cohomology set. We want our definition of descent type to entail the following features.

•

The class
\eta
is the obstruction to lifting
f
to a torsor
Y\to X
under a
k
-form
G
of
\bar{G}
.

•

Two
X
-torsors of the same descent type
\lambda
differ by twisting by a Galois cocycle.

•

If
X(k)\neq\varnothing
, then any descent type can be lifted to a torsor.

In the original descent theory of Colliot-Thélène–Sansuc
[
CTS87
]
, the object having the above properties is the
type
. There, they consider torsors under a torus
T
, with the assumption
\bar{k}[X]^{\times}=\bar{k}^{\times}
on
X
. A type is then a Galois equivariant homomorphism from the character module of
T
to the geometric Picard group
X
, or equivalently, an element of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(X\times_{k}\bar{k},T\times_{k}\bar{k}))
. Later in
[
HS13
]
, Harari and Skorobogatov developped an “open descent theory” for torsors under a group of multiplicative type, without any assumption on the invertible functions on
X
. There, they introduced the notion of
extended type
, which is a morphism in the derived category of Galois modules. Very recently in
[
HW22
]
, Harpaz and Wittenberg deployed the notion of
finite descent type
for their work on supersolvable descent, which is the first non-abelian definition. The descent type constructed in this paper unifies these three definitions.

1.2
Notations and conventions

The followings conventions shall be deployed throughout the article.

Fields.
For most of the cases, we shall consider a perfect field
k
and a fixed algebraic closure
\bar{k}
of it. We denote by
\Gamma_{k}
the absolute Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(\bar{k}/k)
. By convention, a
k
-variety
a separated
k
-scheme of finite type. When
U
is a
k
-scheme, we denote by
X_{U}
the base change
X\times_{k}U
. We usually write
\bar{X}:=X\times_{k}\bar{k}
(but the notation
\bar{X}
does not always mean
X
is the base change to
\bar{k}
of a
k
-variety). For each
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, we have an
X
-automorphism
\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}\operatorname{Spec}(s^{-1}):\bar{X}\to\bar{X}
, which is usually denoted by
s_{\#}
. It induces a bijection on the set
\bar{X}(S)
for any
\bar{k}
-scheme
S
, denoted by
x\mapsto\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-6.9427pt{s}\kern 4.53473pt}}_{{\kern-11.99579pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.53473pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-6.9427pt{s}\kern 4.53473pt}}_{{\kern-11.99579pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.53473pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-4.55222pt{s}\kern 2.80048pt}}_{{\kern-8.16158pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.80048pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-3.75208pt{s}\kern 2.00034pt}}_{{\kern-7.36143pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.00034pt}}}
.

Cohomology.
Unless stated otherwise, every (hyper-)cohomology groups (and sets) shall be
étale
or
Galois
. By abuse of notation, any object of an abelian category is identified to a 1-term complex concentrated in degree
0
. When
k
is a field, every sheaf of abelian groups
\mathcal{F}
on the small étale site
\operatorname{Spec}(k)_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}
shall be abusively identified to the
\Gamma_{k}
-module
\mathcal{F}(\bar{k}):=\varinjlim_{K}\mathcal{F}(K)
, where
K
runs through the finite (or equivalently, finite Galois) extensions of
k
. We also use
\mathcal{D}^{+}(k)
to denote the derived category of bounded below
\Gamma_{k}
-modules.

Torsors.
Let
G
be a
smooth
algebraic group over a perfect field
k
. If
X
is a non-empty
k
-variety, a (right)
X
-torsor under
G
is a
k
-variety
Y
equipped with an action
Y\times_{k}G\to Y
as well as an fppf and
G
-invariant morphism
f:Y\to X
, such that
G(\bar{k})
acts simply transitively on the fibres
f^{-1}(x)
for all
x\in X(\bar{k})
. Such a torsor gives rise to a class
[Y]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)
in the pointed
\check{\textrm{C}}
ech cohomology set for the étale topology (see
[
Mil80
, Chapter III, §4]
or
[
Sko01
, p. 18]
), and
[Y]=[Y^{\prime}]
if and only if
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
are (
G
-equivariantly) isomorphic. For
g\in G(\bar{k})
, we usually denote by
\rho_{g}:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}
the
\bar{X}
-automorphism
y\mapsto y\cdot g
. Note that this automorphism is
not
necessarily
\bar{G}
-equivariant unless
G
is commutative. For
X=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
, the set
\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)
can be described in terms of cocycles as follows. Equip
G(\bar{k})
with the discrete topology. A cocycle
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G)
with coefficients in
G
is a continuous (
i.e.
, locally constant) map
\sigma:\Gamma_{k}\to G(\bar{k})
such that
\sigma_{st}=\sigma_{s}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 4.63394pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-12.095pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.63394pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 4.63394pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-12.095pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.63394pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-4.55167pt{s}\kern 2.79993pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.16103pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.79993pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-3.75168pt{s}\kern 1.99994pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-7.36104pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.99994pt{t}}}}
for all
s,t\in\Gamma_{k}
. Two such cocycles
\sigma
and
\tau
are cohomologous (
i.e.
, define the same element of
\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)
) if and only if there exists
g\in G(\bar{k})
such that
\tau_{s}=g^{-1}\sigma_{s}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.56042pt{s}\kern 4.15245pt}}_{{\kern-11.61351pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.15245pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.56042pt{s}\kern 4.15245pt}}_{{\kern-11.61351pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.15245pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-4.26468pt{s}\kern 2.51294pt}}_{{\kern-7.87404pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.51294pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.54669pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-7.15605pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}
for all
s\in\Gamma_{k}
.

Twisted forms.
Let
k
and
G
be as above. For each cocycle
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G)
, we may
twist

G
to obtain an inner
k
-form
G^{\sigma}
as follows. As an algebraic
\bar{k}
-group,
\bar{G}^{\sigma}:=\bar{G}
, but the twisted action
\cdot_{\sigma}
of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{G}^{\sigma}
is given by
s\mapsto\operatorname{inn}(\sigma_{s})\circ s_{\#}
(here,
\operatorname{inn}(g)
denotes the conjugation by
g
, for each
g\in G(\bar{k})
), that is

\forall s\in\Gamma_{k},\forall g\in\bar{G},\quad s\cdot_{\sigma}g:=\sigma_{s}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.56042pt{s}\kern 4.15245pt}}_{{\kern-11.61351pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.15245pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.56042pt{s}\kern 4.15245pt}}_{{\kern-11.61351pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.15245pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-4.26468pt{s}\kern 2.51294pt}}_{{\kern-7.87404pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.51294pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.54669pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-7.15605pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}\sigma_{s}^{-1}.

If
f:Y\to X
is a torsor under
G
, we define the twisted torsor
f^{\sigma}:Y^{\sigma}\to X
as follows. As a
\bar{k}
-variety,
\bar{Y}^{\sigma}:=\bar{Y}
, and as a
\bar{k}
-morphism,
f^{\sigma}\times_{k}\bar{k}=f\times_{k}\bar{k}
. The Galois action
\cdot_{\sigma}
on
\bar{Y}^{\sigma}
is given by
s\mapsto\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}
, that is,

\forall s\in\Gamma_{k},\forall y\in\bar{Y},\quad s\cdot_{\sigma}y:=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}y^{{\kern-6.71472pt{s}\kern 4.30675pt}}_{{\kern-11.7678pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.30675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}y^{{\kern-6.71472pt{s}\kern 4.30675pt}}_{{\kern-11.7678pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.30675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}y^{{\kern-4.32991pt{s}\kern 2.57817pt}}_{{\kern-7.93927pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.57817pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}y^{{\kern-3.59328pt{s}\kern 1.84154pt}}_{{\kern-7.20264pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.84154pt}}}\cdot\sigma_{s}^{-1}.

If
G
is commutative, then
G^{\sigma}=G
, and
[Y^{\sigma}]=[Y]-p^{\ast}[\sigma]
in
\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)
, where
p:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
denotes the structure morphism.

Groups of multiplicative type.
Let
k
be a field. A
group of multiplicative type

M
over
k
is a
k
-form of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\times\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mu_{n_{i}}
, where
n\geq 0
and
n_{i}\geq 1
. A
k
-
torus
is a connected group of multiplicative type, that is, a
k
-form of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
for some
n\geq 0
. The
character module
of
M
is
\widehat{M}:=\mathscr{H}\kern-0.5ptom_{k}(M,\mathbb{G}_{m})
, that is,
\widehat{M}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\bar{k}}(\bar{M},\mathbb{G}_{m})
as a (finitely generated) abelian group, equipped with the action of
\Gamma_{k}
given by the formula

\forall s\in\Gamma_{k},\forall\chi\in\widehat{M},\forall m\in\bar{M},\quad(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-7.40451pt{s}\kern 4.99654pt}}_{{\kern-12.4576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.99654pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-7.40451pt{s}\kern 4.99654pt}}_{{\kern-12.4576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.99654pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-4.81763pt{s}\kern 3.06589pt}}_{{\kern-8.42699pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.06589pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-3.94165pt{s}\kern 2.18991pt}}_{{\kern-7.55101pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.18991pt}}})(m):=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-7.40451pt{s}\kern 4.99654pt}}_{{\kern-12.4576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.99654pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-7.40451pt{s}\kern 4.99654pt}}_{{\kern-12.4576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.99654pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-4.81763pt{s}\kern 3.06589pt}}_{{\kern-8.42699pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.06589pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-3.94165pt{s}\kern 2.18991pt}}_{{\kern-7.55101pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.18991pt}}}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s^{-1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}m^{{\kern-12.92075pt{s^{-1}}\kern 7.09612pt}}_{{\kern-26.35713pt\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.09612pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s^{-1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}m^{{\kern-12.92075pt{s^{-1}}\kern 7.09612pt}}_{{\kern-26.35713pt\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 7.09612pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s^{-1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}m^{{\kern-9.47066pt{s^{-1}}\kern 4.30226pt}}_{{\kern-18.66333pt\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.30226pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s^{-1}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}m^{{\kern-8.24144pt{s^{-1}}\kern 3.07304pt}}_{{\kern-17.43411pt\mathchoice{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.35765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.71352pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.05728pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.07304pt}}}).

The group
M
is smooth precisely when the integers
n_{i}
are not divisible by the characteristic of
k
. If we denote by
\Delta:\bar{k}[M]\to\bar{k}[M]\otimes_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}[M]
the comultiplication of the Hopf algebra
\bar{k}[M]
, then the
\bar{k}
-vector space
\bar{k}[M]
has a basis consisting of the invertible functions
f\in\bar{k}[M]^{\times}
satisfying
\Delta(f)=f\otimes f
(the
group-like
elements). The
\Gamma_{k}
-module of group-like elements of
\bar{k}[M]
is isomorphic to
\widehat{M}
. Finally, we recall that the construction
M\mapsto\widehat{M}
establishes an exact anti-equivalence of categories, called
Cartier duality
, between the
k
-groups of multiplicative type and the discrete
\Gamma_{k}
-modules which are finitely generated as abelian groups.

1.3
Statements of the main results

The article is organized as follows.

In §
2
, we give the definition of descent type. Paragraph
2.1
recalls the notion of kernel and non-abelian Galois
2
-cohomology, which is crucial to our construction. The definition of descent type and its three fundamental properties shall be presented in paragraph
2.2
. In paragraph
2.3
, we show that the set of descent types on a base variety
X
bound by a kernel
L
is contravariantly functorial in
X
and partially convariantly functoriality in
L
. Unlike torsors, descent types also behave nicely with extension of algebraic groups; this property shall be mentioned in paragraph
2.4
.

The §
3
of this article is devoted to the study of descent types bound by a commutative algebraic group. Our main result here is

Theorem A
(Theorem
3.7
).

Let
k
be a perfect field,
X
a non-empty
k
-variety, and
G
a smooth commutative algebraic
k
-group. There exists a structure of abelian group on the set
\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
of descent types on
X
bound by
\mathbf{lien}(G)
, which is functorial in
X
and
G
(see Definition
2.1

(iii)
). Furthermore, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G),}

(1.1)

where the bottom row is the exact sequence issued from the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence

\mathrm{H}^{p}(k,\mathrm{H}^{q}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(X,G).

The top row of (
1.1
) is a complex, which is exact whenever
X
is quasi-projective, reduced, and geometrically connected.

The top row of (
1.1
) is similar to the fundamental exact sequence of Colliot-Thélène–Sansuc (see
[
Sko01
, Theorem 2.3.6, Corollary 2.3.9]
), or more generally, the exact sequence for the extended types
à la
Harari–Skorobogatov
[
HS13
, Proposition 8.1]
. In fact, we shall show in §
4
that they are equivalent. In doing so, we also give an explicit and accessible description of the extended type in paragraph
4.1
.

Theorem B
(Theorem
4.1
).

For torsors under groups of multiplicative types, the notions of descent type and extended type
[
HS13
, Definition 8.2]
are equivalent.

-cohomology.

2
Definition and basic properties

2.1
Kernels and non-abelian 2-cohomology

In the last 30 years, the notion of kernel
1
1

1

or band, or lien.
and non-abelian Galois
2
-cohomology has been systematically being used for the study of homogeneous spaces without rational points. We recall their definitions and essential properties in this §. The typical references for this subject are
[
Bor93
, §1 and §2]
,
[
FSS98
, §1]
, or
[
DLA19
, §2.2]
.

Let
k
be a
perfect
field. We shall deploy the notion of
k
-kernel in the sense of Borovoi–Springer. Let
p:\bar{X}\to\operatorname{Spec}(\bar{k})
be a non-empty
\bar{k}
-variety. For
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, we shall say that a
k
-automorphism
\phi:\bar{X}\to\bar{X}
is
s
-semilinear
if
p\circ\phi=\operatorname{Spec}(s^{-1})\circ p
. A
1
-semilinear
k
-automorphism is simply a
\bar{k}
-automorphism. In general, to give an
s
-semilinear automorphism of
\bar{X}
is to give a
\bar{k}
-isomorphism
(\operatorname{Spec}(s))^{\ast}\bar{X}\to\bar{X}
. For example, if
X
is a
k
-form of
\bar{X}
, then the action of
s
on
\bar{X}=X\times_{k}\bar{k}
,
i.e.
,
s_{\#}=\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}\operatorname{Spec}(s^{-1})
, is
s
-semilinear. We say that a
k
-automorphism of
\bar{X}
is
semilinear
if it is
s
-semilinear for some
s\in\Gamma_{k}
; such an
s
is necessarily unique. Thus, if
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{X}/k)
denotes the group of semilinear
k
-automorphisms of
\bar{X}
, then we have a homomorphism
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{X}/k)\to\Gamma_{k}
with kernel
\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{X}/\bar{k})
.

Let
\bar{G}
be a
smooth
algebraic group over
\bar{k}
. Let
\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
denote the subgroup of
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{G}/k)
consisting of semilinear automorphisms
\phi:\bar{G}\to\bar{G}
that correspond to an isomorphism

(\operatorname{Spec}(s))^{\ast}\bar{G}\to\bar{G}

of algebraic
\bar{k}
-groups for some (unique)
s\in\Gamma_{k}
. Equivalently, an element
\phi\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{G}/k)
belongs to
\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
if and only if it induces an isomorphism on the abstract group
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
. It is clear that
\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)\cap\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{G}/\bar{k})=\operatorname{Aut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/\bar{k})
, the group of automorphism of the algebraic
\bar{k}
-group
\bar{G}
. Thus, we have an exact sequence

1\to\operatorname{Aut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/\bar{k})\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)\to\Gamma_{k}.

(2.1)

Let
\operatorname{Inn}(\bar{G}):=\{\operatorname{inn}(g):g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})\}
, and put
\operatorname{Out}(\bar{G}):=\operatorname{Aut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/\bar{k})/\operatorname{Inn}(\bar{G})
and
\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}/k):=\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)/\operatorname{Inn}(\bar{G})
, then the sequence

1\to\operatorname{Out}(\bar{G})\to\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}/k)\to\Gamma_{k}

(2.2)

is exact. Equip the set
\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
with the initial topology defined by the evaluation maps

\operatorname{ev}_{g}:\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)\to G(\bar{k}),\quad\phi\mapsto\phi(g)

for
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
, where
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
is given the discrete topology. A
k
-kernel on
\bar{G}
is a homomorphic section
\kappa:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}/k)
of (
2.2
) (
i.e.
, a semilinear outer action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{G}
), which lifts to a continuous (set-theoretic) section
\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
of (
2.1
).

We also call the pair
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
a
k
-kernel. A
k
-form
G
of
\bar{G}
defines a
k
-kernel, denoted by
\mathbf{lien}(G)
. We shall say
G
is a
k
-form of
L
if
L=\mathbf{lien}(G)
. By Galois descent, to give a
k
-form of
G
of
L
is to give a continuous homomorphic section
\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
of (
2.1
) (
i.e.
, a semilinear action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{G}
) lifting
\kappa
. If
G^{\prime}
is another
k
-form of
\bar{G}
, then
G^{\prime}
is a
k
-form of
\mathbf{lien}(G)
if and only if
G^{\prime}
is an inner form of
G
. When
\bar{G}
is commutative, any
k
-kernel on
\bar{G}
has a
unique

k
-form.

There exist at least three equivalent definitions of the set
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
of non-abelian Galois
2
-cohomology with coefficients in
L
. We shall mainly use the one in terms of group extensions. A topological extension of
\Gamma_{k}
by
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
is an exact sequence of topological groups

1\to\bar{G}(\bar{k})\to E\xrightarrow{q}\Gamma_{k}\to 1,

where
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
is discrete in
E
and where the map
q
is open (and continuous). It naturally induces an outer action
\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{Out}(\bar{G}(\bar{k}))=\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{G}(\bar{k}))/\operatorname{Inn}(\bar{G}(\bar{k}))
on the abstract group
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
. We say that such an extension is
bound by
L
if this outer action coincides with the natural outer action induced by
\kappa
. Two such extensions
E
and
E^{\prime}
are said to be
equivalent
if there is an isomorphism
E\xrightarrow{\cong}E^{\prime}
of topological groups fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Let
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
be the set of equivalent classes of topological extensions of
\Gamma_{k}
by
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
which are bound by
L
. It might happen that the set
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
is empty
[
FSS98
, §1.20 and §1.21]
. The class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
of an extension
E
is said to be
neutral
if it is split,
i.e.
,
E\to\Gamma_{k}
has a continuous homomorphic section. Even if
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
is non-empty, it needs not have a neutral element; and if a neutral element exists, it needs not be unique. When
G
is a commutative algebraic
k
-group,
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G):=\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,\mathbf{lien}(G))
is the usual Galois
2
-cohomology group with coefficients in
G
, and
0
is its unique neutral element.

If
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
and
L^{\prime}=(\bar{G}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
are
k
-kernels, a
morphism of
k
-kernels

L\to L^{\prime}
is a morphism
\varphi:\bar{G}\to\bar{G}^{\prime}
of algebraic
\bar{k}
-groups such that there exist respective continuous set-theoretic sections
\phi:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
and
\phi^{\prime}:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}^{\prime}/k)
lifting
\kappa
and
\kappa^{\prime}
, such that
\varphi\circ\phi_{s}=\phi_{s}^{\prime}\circ\varphi
for all
s\in\Gamma_{k}
. The morphism
\varphi
does not necessarily induce a map
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime})
. Nevertheless, there is always a
relation

\varphi_{\ast}:\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)\multimap\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime}),

defined as follows (see
[
DLA19
, §2.2.3]
). A class
\eta^{\prime}\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime})
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with another class
\eta\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
if and only if there exist topological extensions
E
and
E^{\prime}
representing
\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
respectively, and a continuous homomorphism
E\to E^{\prime}
fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

If either the underlying morphism
\varphi:\bar{G}\to\bar{G}^{\prime}
of algebraic
\bar{k}
-groups is surjective or
\bar{G}^{\prime}
is commutative, then the relation
\varphi_{\ast}
is in fact a
map
. We also note that, if
K
is any field extension of
k
which is perfect, then
L
restricts to a
K
-kernel
L_{K}
, and we have a restriction map

\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(K,L):=\mathrm{H}^{2}(K,L_{K}).

2.2
Non-abelian descent types

We consider a perfect field
k
and a non-empty variety
p:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
. For most of the cases,
X
will be quasi-projective (a condition which allows Galois descent).

If
\bar{Y}
is a
\bar{k}
variety equipped with a morphism
f:\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}:=X_{\bar{k}}
, we set
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X):=\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/X)\cap\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/k)
. This group is equipped with the
weak topology
, which, by definition, is the initial topology defined by the maps

\operatorname{ev}_{y}:\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)\to\bar{Y}(\bar{k}),\quad\alpha\mapsto\alpha(y),

for
y\in\bar{Y}(\bar{k})
. One can then show that the restriction
q:\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)\to\Gamma_{k}
of the natural homomorphism
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/k)\to\Gamma_{k}
is open. Nevertheless,
q
needs not be continuous. For technical reasons, we shall work with a finer topology on
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
, called the
modified weak topology
. It is, by definition, the initial topology defined by the maps
\operatorname{ev}_{y}
and
q
. This makes
q
continuous and open without changing the subspace topology on
\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X})
(which equals
\operatorname{Ker}(q)
). Indeed, to show that
q
is open, consider a basic open subset
U\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
, which has the form

U=\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{ev}_{y_{i}}^{-1}(\Sigma_{i})\right)\cap q^{-1}(s_{0}\Gamma_{K})=\{\alpha\in q^{-1}(s_{0}\Gamma_{K}):\forall i\in\{1,\ldots,n\},\alpha(y_{i})\in\Sigma_{i}\},

where
n\geq 0
,
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\in\bar{Y}(\bar{k})
,
\Sigma_{1},\ldots,\Sigma_{n}\subseteq\bar{Y}(\bar{k})
,
s_{0}\in\Gamma_{k}
, and
K/k
is a finite extension. Take a sufficiently large finite extension
K^{\prime}/K
such that
\bar{Y}
is the base change to
\bar{k}
of a torsor
Y\to X_{K^{\prime}}
under a
K^{\prime}
-form
G^{\prime}
of
\bar{G}
, and
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}\in Y(K^{\prime})
. Let
\alpha\in U
. For each
t\in\Gamma_{K^{\prime}}
, let
t_{\#}:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}
denote the action of
t
on
\bar{Y}
defined
via
its
K^{\prime}
-form
Y
. Since
t_{\#}\in\Gamma_{K^{\prime}}\subseteq\Gamma_{K}
stabilizes
y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}
, one has
\alpha\circ t_{\#}\in U
. It follows that
q(\alpha)t=q(\alpha\circ t_{\#})\in q(U)
, hence
q(U)\subseteq\Gamma_{k}
contains the open neighborhood
q(\alpha)\Gamma_{K^{\prime}}
of
q(\alpha)
. Since
\alpha
is arbitrary, we conclude that
q(U)
is open. Furthermore, a set-theoretic section
\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
is continuous with respect to this modified topology if and only if it is continuous with respect to the weak topology.

To sum up, we have an exact sequence of topological groups

1\to\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X})\to\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)\xrightarrow{q}\Gamma_{k}.

(2.3)

If
f:\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
is a torsor under a smooth algebraic group
\bar{G}
over
\bar{k}
, then we have an inclusion

\bar{G}(\bar{X})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X}),\quad\sigma\mapsto\rho_{\sigma}^{-1},

where, for each
\bar{k}
-morphism
\sigma:\bar{X}\to\bar{G}
, the
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
\rho_{\sigma}:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}
is defined by
y\mapsto y\cdot\sigma(f(y))
. In particular, we have an inclusion

\bar{G}(\bar{k})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X}),\quad g\mapsto\rho_{g}^{-1},

Note that the topology on
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
induces the discrete topology on
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
. Indeed, fix a point
y\in\bar{Y}(\bar{k})
, then its stabilizer
U\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
is an open subset and
U\cap\bar{G}(\bar{k})=\{1\}
by the very definition of a torsor.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
be a
k
-kernel.

(i).

A
descent datum on
X
bound by
L
is a pair
(\bar{Y},E)
, where
\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
is a torsor under
\bar{G}
and
E\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
is a subgroup fitting in an exact sequence

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g\mapsto\rho_{g}^{-1}}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}}

of topological groups, where the top row is bound by
L
and the bottom row is (
2.3
).

(ii).

Let
L^{\prime}=(\bar{G}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
be a second
k
-kernel,
\varphi:L\to L^{\prime}
a morphism, and
(\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime})
a descent datum on
X
bound by
L^{\prime}
. A
morphism of descent data
(\bar{Y},E)\to(\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime})
compatible with
\varphi
is a pair
(\iota,\varpi)
, where
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
is an
\varphi
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-morphism and
\varpi:E\to E^{\prime}
is a continuous homomorphism fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

such that the diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}^{\prime}}

commutes for all
\alpha\in E
.

(iii).

An isomorphism of descent data on
X
bound by
L
is an isomorphism compatible with
\operatorname{id}_{L}
. The isomorphism class of such a descent datum
(\bar{Y},E)
is denoted by
[\bar{Y},E]
and is called a
descent type on
X
bound by
L
. Denote by
\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
the set of such isomorphism classes.

It is easy to see that an isomorphism
(\bar{Y},E)\xrightarrow{\cong}(\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime})
of descent data on
X
bound by
L
is given by a
\bar{G}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
such that
\iota\circ\alpha\circ\iota^{-1}\in E^{\prime}
for all
\alpha\in E
.

Remarks 2.2
.

Let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
be a
k
-kernel.

(i).

For each
s
-semilinear automorphism
\phi\in\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
, where
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, one defines the
conjugate torsor

\phi\bar{Y}
over
\bar{X}
as follows. Let
\phi\bar{Y}
be the
\bar{X}
-scheme
\bar{Y}
equipped with the “twisted” structure morphism
\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}\xrightarrow{s_{\#}}\bar{X}
. Then the action

\bar{Y}\times\bar{G}\to\bar{Y},\quad(y,g)\mapsto y\cdot\phi^{-1}(g)

makes
\phi\bar{Y}
an
\bar{X}
-torsor under
\bar{G}
. This construction yields action of
\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
on the set
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})
. If
\phi=\operatorname{inn}(g)
for some
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
, then the map
\rho_{g}
defines a
\bar{G}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
\bar{Y}\xrightarrow{\cong}\phi\bar{Y}
, that is,
[\bar{Y}]=[\phi\bar{Y}]
in
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})
. Thus, we obtain an action of
\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}/k)
on
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})
. In particular, the map
\kappa
naturally induces an action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})
. If
(\bar{Y},E)
is descent datum on
X
bound by
L
, then the surjectivity of
E\to\Gamma_{k}
implies
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
. Thus, we obtain two maps

\operatorname{DType}(X,L)\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})),\quad[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[\bar{Y}]

and

\operatorname{DType}(X,L)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L),\quad[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[E].

(ii).

Suppose that
(\bar{Y},E)
is a descent datum on
X
bound by
L
with
\bar{Y}
connected and
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
finite. Then
\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X})=\bar{G}(\bar{k})
, hence
E=\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}/X)
. Thus, the descent type
[\bar{Y},E]
is completely determined by the
\bar{X}
-torsor
\bar{Y}
under
\bar{G}
, which is a finite étale cover of
\bar{X}
such that the composite
\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}\to X
is Galois (that is, the field extension
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})/k(X)
is Galois). We recover the notion of
finite descent type
by Harpaz–Wittenberg
[
HW22
, Definition 2.1]
.

A descent type
[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
gives rise to not only an
\bar{X}
-torsor
\bar{Y}
, but also a cohomology class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
. Let us show that the non-neutrality this class is precisely the obstruction to lifting
\bar{Y}
to an
X
-torsor
Y
and that two such liftings differ by twisting by a Galois cocycle.

Let
Y\to X
be a torsor under an algebraic
k
-group
G
. Then we have a semilinear action of
\Gamma_{k}
of
Y
, that is, a continuous homomorphic section

\varsigma_{Y}:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X),\quad s\mapsto s_{\#}.

It is easily checked that

\forall s\in\Gamma_{k},\forall g\in G(\bar{k}),\quad\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.18906pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-5.76718pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-2.67621pt{s}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-5.25433pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}}=s_{\#}\circ\rho_{g}\circ s_{\#}^{-1}.

(2.4)

Let
E_{Y}\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
denote the semidirect product of
G(\bar{k})
and
\varsigma_{Y}(\Gamma_{k})
. Then the pair
(\bar{Y},E_{Y})
is a descent datum on
X
bound by
\mathbf{lien}(G)
and the cohomology class
[E_{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,\mathbf{lien}(G))
is neutral. The converse also holds under mild conditions.

Definition 2.3
.

The
descent type
of the torsor
Y\to X
is

\mathbf{dtype}(Y):=[\bar{Y},E_{Y}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,\mathbf{lien}(G)).

Proposition 2.4
.

Suppose that
X
is quasi-projective, reduced, and geometrically connected. Let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
be a
k
-kernel and
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
a descent type on
X
bound by
L
(in particular,
\bar{Y}
is quasi-projective).

(i).

There is a canonical bijection between the continuous homomorphic sections
\Gamma_{k}\to E
and the equivalence classes of pair
(Y,G)
, where
G
is a
k
-form of
L
(that is,
\mathbf{lien}(G)=L
) and
Y\to X
is a torsor under
G
of descent type
\mathbf{dtype}(Y)=\lambda
. Here, we impose that two pairs
(Y,G)
and
(Y^{\prime},G^{\prime})
to be equivalent if
G^{\prime}=G
and the
X
-torsors
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
under
G
are isomorphic. In particular,
X
-torsors of descent type
\lambda
exist if and only if the class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
is neutral.

(ii).

If
X(k)\neq\varnothing
, then the class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
is neutral.

(iii).

Let
Y\to X
be a torsor under a
k
-form
G
of
L
, of descent type
\mathbf{dtype}(Y)=\lambda
. If
Y^{\prime}\to X
is a torsor under a
k
-form
G^{\prime}
of
L
, then
\mathbf{dtype}(Y^{\prime})=\lambda
if and only if there exists a Galois cocycle
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G)
such that
G^{\prime}=G^{\sigma}
and the
X
-torsors
Y^{\prime}
and
Y^{\sigma}
under
G^{\sigma}
are isomorphic.

Proof.

(i).

As we have seen above, a torsor
Y\to X
under a
k
-form
G
of
L
defines a continuous homomorphic section
\varsigma_{Y}:\Gamma_{k}\to E
(here, we abusively identity
\bar{Y}
and
\bar{G}
to the respective base changes
Y_{\bar{k}}
and
G_{\bar{k}}
, hence
E_{Y}=E
whenever
\mathbf{dtype}(Y)=\lambda
). Conversely, as in the proof of
[
HS02
, Proposition 2.2 (3)]
, a continuous homomorphic section
\Gamma_{k}\to E
gives rise to a torsor
Y\to X
under a
k
-form
G
of
L
(in
loc. cit.
, the authors use the weak topology on
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
instead of our modified weak topology, but we have seen that this does not matter). One checks that these two constructions are converse to each other.

(ii).

See
[
HS02
, Theorem 2.5]
.

(iii).

We make the following identifications:
\bar{Y}=Y_{\bar{k}}
,
\bar{G}=G_{\bar{k}}
, and
E=E_{Y}
, the semidirect product of
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
and
\varsigma_{Y}(\Gamma_{k})=\{s_{\#}:s\in\Gamma_{k}\}
inside
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
(as always,
s\mapsto s_{\#}
denotes the semilinear action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{Y}
defined
via
the
k
-form
Y
). If
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G)
is any Galois cocycle, then clearly
\bar{Y}^{\sigma}=\bar{Y}
and
\mathbf{lien}(G^{\sigma})=\mathbf{lien}(G)=L
. Furthermore, the twisted action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{Y}^{\sigma}
is
s\mapsto\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}
. Thus,
E_{Y^{\sigma}}\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
is the semidirect product of
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
and
\varsigma_{Y^{\sigma}}(\Gamma_{k})=\{\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}:s\in\Gamma_{k}\}
, which is visibly equal to
E_{Y}
. Hence,
\mathbf{dtype}(Y^{\sigma})=\lambda
.

Conversely, let
Y^{\prime}\to X
be a torsor under a
k
-form
G^{\prime}
of
L
, with
\mathbf{dtype}(Y^{\prime})=\lambda
. By definition, there exists a
\bar{G}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
such that the diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha\mapsto\iota\circ\alpha\circ\iota^{-1}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{\prime}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

(2.5)

commutes. Write
(s,g)\mapsto\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.56042pt{s}\kern 4.15245pt}}_{{\kern-11.61351pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.15245pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.56042pt{s}\kern 4.15245pt}}_{{\kern-11.61351pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.15245pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-4.26468pt{s}\kern 2.51294pt}}_{{\kern-7.87404pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.51294pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.54669pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-7.15605pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}

for the action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
induced by the
k
-form
G
.
Unwinding the commutativity of the left square of (
2.5
) yields

\forall g\in G(\bar{k}),\quad\rho^{\prime}_{g}=\iota\circ\rho_{g}\circ\iota^{-1}.

(2.6)

where
\rho^{\prime}_{g}:\bar{Y}^{\prime}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
is the
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
y^{\prime}\mapsto y^{\prime}\cdot g
. Write
s\mapsto s_{\#}^{\prime}
for the action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{Y}^{\prime}
. That the right square of (
2.5
) commutes implies that
s_{\#}
and
\iota^{-1}\circ s^{\prime}_{\#}\circ\iota
are both mapped to
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, so that we may write

\iota^{-1}\circ s^{\prime}_{\#}\circ\iota=\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}

(2.7)

for some (unique)
\sigma_{s}\in G(\bar{k})
. The map
s\mapsto\sigma_{s}
is continuous. Let us verify that it is a cocycle. Indeed, let

. Since
(st)^{\prime}_{\#}=s^{\prime}_{\#}\circ t^{\prime}_{\#}
, it follows from (
2.7
) that

\rho_{\sigma_{st}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}\circ t_{\#}=\rho_{\sigma_{st}}^{-1}\circ(st)_{\#}=\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}\circ\rho_{\sigma_{t}}^{-1}\circ t_{\#},

or
\rho_{\sigma_{st}}^{-1}=\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}\circ\rho_{\sigma_{t}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}^{-1}=\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-5.9387pt{s}\kern
