1.99994pt\mathchoice{\makebox[2.81831pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.81831pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.90277pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-5.97217pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.99994pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-2.82263pt{s}\kern 1.42853pt\mathchoice{\makebox[2.81831pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.81831pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.90277pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-5.40076pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.42853pt{t}}}}}^{-1}=\rho_{\sigma_{s}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-5.9387pt{s}\kern 4.07585pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.09288pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-9.54807pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.07585pt{\tau}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-5.9387pt{s}\kern 4.07585pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.09288pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-9.54807pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.07585pt{\tau}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-3.39404pt{s}\kern 1.99994pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.09288pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-5.97217pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.99994pt{\tau}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-2.82263pt{s}\kern 1.42853pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.40804pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.53003pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.09288pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-5.40076pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.42853pt{\tau}}}}}^{-1}
, in view of (
2.4
). Hence,
\sigma_{st}=\sigma_{s}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 4.63394pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-12.095pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.63394pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 4.63394pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-12.095pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.63394pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-4.55167pt{s}\kern 2.79993pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.16103pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.79993pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-3.75168pt{s}\kern 1.99994pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.02084pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76944pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-7.36104pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.99994pt{t}}}}
, or
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G)
. Now, let us show that
G^{\prime}=G^{\sigma}
. Indeed, let
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and
g\in G(\bar{k})
, then

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{s\cdot_{\sigma}g}

\displaystyle=\iota\circ\rho_{s\cdot_{\sigma}g}\circ\iota^{-1},

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:DescentType2}},

\displaystyle=\iota\circ\rho_{\sigma_{s}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.18906pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-5.76718pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-2.67621pt{s}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-5.25433pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}\sigma_{s}^{-1}}\circ\iota^{-1},

\displaystyle\text{by definition of $\cdot_{\sigma}$},

\displaystyle=(\iota\circ\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1})\circ\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.18906pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-5.76718pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-2.67621pt{s}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-5.25433pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}}\circ(\rho_{\sigma_{s}}\circ\iota^{-1})

\displaystyle=(s^{\prime}_{\#}\circ\iota\circ s_{\#}^{-1})\circ(s_{\#}\circ\rho_{g}\circ s_{\#}^{-1})\circ(s_{\#}\circ\iota^{-1}\circ(s^{\prime}_{\#})^{-1}),

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:DescentType1} and \eqref{eq:DescentType3}},

\displaystyle=s^{\prime}_{\#}\circ(\iota\circ\rho_{g}\circ\iota^{-1})\circ(s^{\prime}_{\#})^{-1}

\displaystyle=s^{\prime}_{\#}\circ\rho^{\prime}_{g}\circ(s^{\prime}_{\#})^{-1},

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:DescentType2}}.

This equality means precisely that the action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
induced by the
k
-form
G^{\prime}
is given by
(s,g)\mapsto s\cdot_{\sigma}g
, or
G^{\prime}=G^{\sigma}
. Finally, for each
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, the diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\prime}_{\#}}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}^{\prime}}

commutes, in view of (
2.7
). By the very definition of twisting,
s\mapsto\rho_{\sigma_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}
is just the action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{Y}
defined
via
the
k
-form
Y^{\sigma}
. Thus,
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
descends into a
G^{\sigma}
-equivariant
X
-isomorphism
Y^{\sigma}\to Y^{\prime}
.

2.3
Functoriality

Let
k
be a perfect field. Naturally, we expect the set
\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
to be functorial in
X
and in
L
.

Construction 2.5
(Pullback of descent types).

Let
f:X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism between non-empty
k
-varieties and let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
be a
k
-kernel. Then
f
induces a pullback map
f^{\ast}:\operatorname{DType}(X,L)\to\operatorname{DType}(X^{\prime},L)
as follows. Let
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
. For each
s
-semilinear automorphism
\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
(where
s\in\Gamma_{k}
), define
f^{\ast}\alpha:=\alpha\times_{\bar{X}}s_{\#}^{\prime}
(where
s_{\#}^{\prime}
denotes the natural semilinear Galois action on
\bar{X}^{\prime}
), then
f^{\ast}\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(f^{\ast}\bar{Y}/X)
(where
f^{\ast}\bar{Y}=\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{X}}\bar{X}^{\prime}
). We obtain a continuous homomorphism
f^{\ast}:\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)\to\operatorname{SAut}(f^{\ast}\bar{Y}/X^{\prime})
. Let
f^{\ast}E
be the image of
E
by
f^{\ast}
, then
f^{\ast}\lambda:=[f^{\ast}\bar{Y},f^{\ast}E]
is a descent type on
X^{\prime}
bound by
L
. It is clear that
[E]=[f^{\ast}E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
. If
Y\to X
is a torsor under a
k
-form
G
of
L
, then
\mathbf{dtype}(f^{\ast}Y)=f^{\ast}\mathbf{dtype}(Y)
. Finally,
\operatorname{id}_{X}^{\ast}=\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{DType}(X,L)}
and
(f\circ f^{\prime})^{\ast}=(f^{\prime})^{\ast}\circ f^{\ast}
for any morphism
f^{\prime}:X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
between non-empty
k
-varieties. We also note that, for any field extension
K/k
(with
K
perfect), a similar construction yields a restriction map
\operatorname{DType}(X,L)\to\operatorname{DType}(X_{K},L_{K})
.

As we have seen in the previous paragraph, “functoriality” in
L
for the non-abelian cohomology set
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
is more delicate and can only defined partially in terms of relations. This is also the case for descent types.

Construction 2.6
(Pushforward of descent types).

Let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
and
L^{\prime}=(\bar{G}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
be
k
-kernels, and let
\varphi:L\to L^{\prime}
be a morphism. We define a relation

\varphi_{\ast}:\operatorname{DType}(X,L)\multimap\operatorname{DType}(X,L^{\prime}),

where a descent type
[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L^{\prime})
is in relation with another descent type
[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
if there exists a morphism of descent data
(\bar{Y},E)\to(\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime})
compatible with
\varphi
in the sense of Definition
2.1

(ii)
. In this case, the class
[E^{\prime}]
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}:\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)\multimap\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime})
with
[E]
. Furthermore,
\operatorname{id}_{L\ast}
is the relation “
=
” on
\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
. If
\varphi^{\prime}:L^{\prime}\to L^{\prime\prime}
is another morphism of
k
-kernels, then
(\varphi^{\prime}\circ\varphi)_{\ast}
is the composite relation of
\varphi^{\prime}_{\ast}
and
\varphi_{\ast}
. If
G
and
G^{\prime}
are respective
k
-forms of
L
and
L^{\prime}
such that
\varphi
descends into a morphism
\varphi:G\to G^{\prime}
of algebraic
k
-groups, and if
Y\to X
is a torsor under
G
, then
\mathbf{dtype}(\varphi_{\ast}Y)
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\mathbf{dtype}(Y)
.

Let us show that, in the case where
\varphi:\bar{G}\to\bar{G}^{\prime}
is surjective or the underlying
\bar{k}
-group
\bar{G^{\prime}}
of
L^{\prime}
is commutative, the relation
\varphi_{\ast}
in Construction
2.6
is in fact a map.

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
and
L^{\prime}=(\bar{G}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
be
k
-kernels, and let
\varphi:L\to L^{\prime}
be a surjective morphism. Let
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
,
\bar{H}:=\operatorname{Ker}(\bar{G}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\bar{G}^{\prime})
, and
\bar{Z}:=\varphi_{\ast}\bar{Y}=\bar{Y}/\bar{H}
. For each
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, any
s
-semilinear automorphism
\alpha\in E
induces a unique
s
-semilinear automorphism
\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/X)
fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ast}\alpha}
\textstyle{\bar{Z},}

where
\pi:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Z}
is the canonical
\varphi
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-morphism. Let
\varphi_{\ast}E:=\{\varphi_{\ast}\alpha:\alpha\in E\}
, then
\varphi_{\ast}\lambda:=[\bar{Z},\varphi_{\ast}E]
is the unique element of
\operatorname{DType}(X,L^{\prime})
which is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\lambda
.

Proof.

Since
E
is an extension of
\Gamma_{k}
by
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
, we have a semilinear action
\psi:E\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
by conjugation in
E
, that is,
\alpha\circ\rho_{g}\circ\alpha^{-1}=\rho_{\psi_{\alpha}(g)}
for all
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
and
\alpha\in E
. That
\varphi
is a morphism of
k
-kernels means there are respective continuous (set-theoretic) liftings
\phi:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
and
\phi^{\prime}:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}^{\prime}/k)
of
\kappa
and
\kappa^{\prime}
such that
\varphi\circ\phi_{s}=\phi^{\prime}_{s}\circ\varphi
for all
s\in\Gamma_{k}
. Let
\alpha\in E
and let
s
be its image in
\Gamma_{k}
, then
\psi_{\alpha}=\operatorname{inn}(g_{0})\circ\phi_{s}
for some
g_{0}\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
. It follows that
\psi_{\alpha}
stabilizes
\bar{H}
. For
y\in\bar{Y}
and
h\in\bar{H}
, we have
\alpha(y\cdot h)=\alpha(y)\cdot\psi_{\alpha}(h)
, hence
\pi(\alpha(y\cdot h))=\pi(\alpha(y))
. It follows that
\alpha
induces an
s
-semilinear automorphism
\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/X)
as claimed. Uniqueness follows from surjectivity of
\pi
. In particular, the formation

E\to\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/X),\quad\alpha\mapsto\varphi_{\ast}\alpha

is a continuous homomorphism. Furthermore, for each
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
, the automorphism
\varphi_{\ast}\rho_{g}:\bar{Z}\to\bar{Z}
coincides with the
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
\rho_{\varphi(g)}
taking
z
to
z\cdot\varphi(g)
. Since
\varphi
is surjective, it follows that
\varphi_{\ast}E
contains
\{\rho_{g^{\prime}}:g^{\prime}\in\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})\}
and that the sequence

1\to\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})\to\varphi_{\ast}E\to\Gamma_{k}\to 1

is exact. In addition, it is immediate from Construction
2.6
that
[\bar{Z},\varphi_{\ast}E]
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\lambda
.

Suppose that
[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L^{\prime})
is a descent type in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\lambda
. By definition, there is an
\varphi
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-morphism
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
and a continuous homomorphism
\varpi:E\to E^{\prime}
fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1,}

such that
\iota\circ\alpha=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\iota
for all
\alpha\in E
. Then
\iota
induces a
\bar{G}^{\prime}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
i:\bar{Z}\xrightarrow{\cong}\bar{Y}^{\prime}
, that is,
\iota=i\circ\pi
. For any
\alpha\in E
, we have

i\circ\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ i^{-1}\circ\iota=i\circ\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\pi=i\circ\pi\circ\alpha=\iota\circ\alpha=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\iota.

Since
\iota
is surjective, it follows that
i\circ\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ i^{-1}=\varpi_{\alpha}\in E^{\prime}
, hence
[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]=[\bar{Z},\varphi_{\ast}E]=\varphi_{\ast}\lambda
.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
be a
k
-kernel,
G^{\prime}
a smooth commutative algebraic
k
-group, and
\varphi:L\to\mathbf{lien}(G^{\prime})
a morphism. Let
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
, and
\bar{Z}:=\varphi_{\ast}\bar{Y}=\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}^{\bar{G}}\bar{G}^{\prime}
, that is, the quotient of
\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}
by the left action of
\bar{G}
defined by
g\cdot(y,g^{\prime}):=(y\cdot g^{-1},\varphi(g)g^{\prime})
. For each
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and each
s
-semilinear automorphism
\alpha\in E
, the automorphism
\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}/X)
induces a unique
s
-semilinear automorphism
\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/X)
fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{y\mapsto(y,1)}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y\mapsto(y,1)}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ast}\alpha}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}}

where
\pi:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Z}
is the canonical projection. Let
\varphi_{\ast}E
be subgroup of
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/X)
generated by
\{\varphi_{\ast}\alpha:\alpha\in E\}
and
\{\rho_{g^{\prime}}:g^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}(\bar{k})\}
, then
\varphi_{\ast}\lambda:=[\bar{Z},\varphi_{\ast}E]
is the unique element of
\operatorname{DType}(X,\mathbf{lien}(G^{\prime}))
which is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\lambda
.

Proof.

Let
\psi_{\alpha}\in\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
be as in the proof of Lemma
2.7
, that is,
\alpha\circ\rho_{g}\circ\alpha^{-1}=\rho_{\psi_{\alpha}(g)}
for all
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
. Using this identity and (
2.4
), we have, for all
g^{\prime}\times\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})
, that

(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#})\circ(\rho_{g}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{\varphi(g)})=(\rho_{\psi_{\alpha}(g)}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-15.5522pt{s}\kern 13.68935pt}}_{{\kern-19.16156pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 13.68935pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-15.5522pt{s}\kern 13.68935pt}}_{{\kern-19.16156pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 13.68935pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-8.20087pt{s}\kern 6.80676pt}}_{{\kern-10.77899pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.80676pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-6.25604pt{s}\kern 4.86194pt}}_{{\kern-8.83417pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.86194pt}}}}))\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}).

Since
\varphi
is a morphism of
k
-kernels, there exists a continuous (set-theoretic) lifting
\phi:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}/k)
such that
\varphi\circ\phi_{t}=t_{\#}\circ\varphi
for all
t\in\Gamma_{k}
. Then
\psi_{\alpha}=\operatorname{inn}(g_{0})\circ\phi_{s}
for some
g_{0}\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
. It follows that
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-18.0702pt{s}\kern 15.66223pt}}_{{\kern-23.12329pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.66223pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-18.0702pt{s}\kern 15.66223pt}}_{{\kern-23.12329pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 15.66223pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-11.28119pt{s}\kern 9.52945pt}}_{{\kern-14.89055pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 9.52945pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi(g)^{{\kern-8.5585pt{s}\kern 6.80676pt}}_{{\kern-12.16786pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.80676pt}}}=\varphi(\phi_{s}(g))=\varphi(g_{0}^{-1}\psi_{\alpha}(g)g_{0})=\varphi(\psi_{\alpha}(g))
since
G^{\prime}
is commutative. Thus,

\pi\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#})\circ(\rho_{g}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{\varphi(g)})=\pi\circ(\rho_{\psi_{\alpha}(g)}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{\psi_{\alpha}(g)}))\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#})=\pi\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}).

From this, we conclude that
\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}
induces an
s
-semilinear automorphism
\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/X)
as stated. Uniqueness follows from surjectivity of
\pi
. In particular, the formation

E\to\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/X),\quad\alpha\mapsto\varphi_{\ast}\alpha

is a continuous homomorphism. Furthermore, for each
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
, the automorphism
\varphi_{\ast}\rho_{g}:\bar{Z}\to\bar{Z}
coincides with the
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
\rho_{\varphi(g)}
taking
z
to
z\cdot\varphi(g)
. Let us show that the sequence

1\to G^{\prime}(\bar{k})\to\varphi_{\ast}E\to\Gamma_{k}\to 1

is exact. Clearly, only exactness at the term
\varphi_{\ast}E
needs to be checked. First, we note that for any
g^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}(\bar{k})
, the diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}_{\bar{Y}}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{g^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{g^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}}

commutes. It follows that, for any
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and any
and any
s
-semilinear automorphism
\alpha\in E
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\rho_{g^{\prime}}\circ\pi

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\pi\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\bar{Y}}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{g^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\pi\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#})\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\bar{Y}}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{g^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\pi\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\bar{Y}}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.18906pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-5.76718pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-2.67621pt{s}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-5.25433pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}^{\prime}})\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}),

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:DescentType1}},

\displaystyle=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.18906pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-5.76718pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-2.67621pt{s}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-5.25433pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}^{\prime}}\circ\pi\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#})

\displaystyle=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.18906pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-5.76718pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-2.67621pt{s}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-5.25433pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\pi,

hence
\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\rho_{g^{\prime}}=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-5.54517pt{s}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-9.15453pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-3.18906pt{s}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-5.76718pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-2.67621pt{s}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-5.25433pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{\ast}\alpha
because
\pi
is surjective. Thus,
\varphi_{\ast}E
normalizes
G^{\prime}(\bar{k})
, that is, any element
\beta\in\varphi_{\ast}E
can be written as
\beta=\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\rho_{g^{\prime}}
for some
\alpha\in E
and
g^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}(\bar{k})
. If the image of
\beta
in
\Gamma_{k}
is
1
, then
\alpha=\rho_{g}
for some
g\in G(\bar{k})
, hence
\beta=\varphi_{\ast}\rho_{g}\circ\rho_{g^{\prime}}=\rho_{g^{\prime}\varphi(g)}
, which is what needed to be proved. It is now immediate from Construction
2.6
that the descent type
[\bar{Z},\varphi_{\ast}E]
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\lambda
.

Suppose that
[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,\mathbf{lien}(G^{\prime}))
is a descent type in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\lambda
. By definition, there is an
\varphi
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-morphism
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
and a continuous homomorphism
\varpi:E\to E^{\prime}
fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{\prime}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1,}

such that
\iota\circ\alpha=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\iota
for all
\alpha\in E
. Then
\iota
induces a
\bar{G}^{\prime}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
i:\bar{Z}\xrightarrow{\cong}\bar{Y}^{\prime}
. Denote by
\gamma:\bar{Y}^{\prime}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
the action of
\bar{G}^{\prime}
, then
i\circ\pi=\gamma\circ(\iota\times_{\bar{k}}\operatorname{id}_{\bar{G}^{\prime}})
. As we have seen above, any element
\beta\in\varphi_{\ast}E
can be written as
\beta=\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\rho_{h}=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-3.41066pt{s}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-5.98878pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-2.83449pt{s}\kern 1.44038pt}}_{{\kern-5.41261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.44038pt}}}}\circ\varphi_{\ast}\alpha
for some
\alpha\in E
and
h\in G^{\prime}(\bar{k})
(where
s\in\Gamma_{k}
is the image of
\alpha
). Since the extension
E^{\prime}
is bound by
\mathbf{lien}(G^{\prime})
, its conjugation action on
\bar{G^{\prime}}
is compatible with the natural Galois action, hence
\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\rho_{h}=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-3.41066pt{s}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-5.98878pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-2.83449pt{s}\kern 1.44038pt}}_{{\kern-5.41261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.44038pt}}}}\circ\varpi_{\alpha}
and
\gamma\circ(\varpi_{\alpha}\times_{\bar{k{}}}s_{\#})=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\gamma
. Thus

\displaystyle i\circ\beta\circ\pi

\displaystyle=(i\circ\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-3.41066pt{s}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-5.98878pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-2.83449pt{s}\kern 1.44038pt}}_{{\kern-5.41261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.44038pt}}}})\circ(\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\circ\pi)

\displaystyle=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-3.41066pt{s}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-5.98878pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-2.83449pt{s}\kern 1.44038pt}}_{{\kern-5.41261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.44038pt}}}}\circ i\circ\pi\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}),

\displaystyle\text{since $i$ is $\bar{G}^{\prime}$-equivariant},

\displaystyle=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-3.41066pt{s}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-5.98878pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-2.83449pt{s}\kern 1.44038pt}}_{{\kern-5.41261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.44038pt}}}}\circ\gamma\circ(\iota\times_{\bar{k}}\operatorname{id}_{\bar{G}^{\prime}})\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#})

\displaystyle=\gamma\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\bar{Y}^{\prime}}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-6.00064pt{s}\kern 4.13779pt}}_{{\kern-9.61pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.13779pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-3.41066pt{s}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-5.98878pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}h^{{\kern-2.83449pt{s}\kern 1.44038pt}}_{{\kern-5.41261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.44038pt}}}})\circ(\iota\times_{\bar{k}}\operatorname{id}_{\bar{G}^{\prime}})\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}s_{\#}),

\displaystyle\text{since }G^{\prime}\text{ is commutative},

\displaystyle=\gamma\circ(\varpi_{\alpha}\times_{\bar{k{}}}s_{\#})\circ(\iota\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{h}),

\displaystyle\text{by the equalities $\iota\circ\alpha=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\iota$ and \eqref{eq:DescentType1}},

\displaystyle=\omega_{\alpha}\circ\gamma\circ(\operatorname{id}_{\bar{Y}^{\prime}}\times_{\bar{k}}\rho_{h})\circ(\iota\times_{\bar{k}}\operatorname{id}_{\bar{G}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\rho_{h}\circ\gamma\circ(\iota\times_{\bar{k}}\operatorname{id}_{\bar{G}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\text{since }G^{\prime}\text{ is commutative},

\displaystyle=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\rho_{h}\circ i\circ\pi.

Since
\pi
is surjective, it follows that
i\circ\beta=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\rho_{h}\circ i
, hence
i\circ\beta\circ i^{-1}=\varpi_{\alpha}\circ\rho_{h}\in E^{\prime}
. This shows that
[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]=[\bar{Z},\varphi_{\ast}E]=\varphi_{\ast}\lambda
.

Lemma 2.9
.

Constructions
2.5
and
2.6
commute. More precisely, let
f:X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism of non-empty
k
-varieties, let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
and
L^{\prime}=(\bar{G}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
be
k
-kernels, and let
\varphi:L\to L^{\prime}
be a morphism. If a descent type
\lambda^{\prime}=[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L^{\prime})
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with a descent type
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
, then
f^{\ast}\lambda^{\prime}\in\operatorname{DType}(X^{\prime},L^{\prime})
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
f^{\ast}\lambda\in\operatorname{DType}(X^{\prime},L)
. In particular, if either
\bar{G}^{\prime}
is commutative or
\varphi
is surjective, then we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X,L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X,L^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X^{\prime},L)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X^{\prime},L^{\prime}).}

Proof.

That
\lambda^{\prime}
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
\lambda
means there exist an
\varphi
-equivariant morphism
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}
and a continuous homomorphism
\varpi:E\to E^{\prime}
satisfying the conditions in Definition
2.1

(ii)
. Recall that there are isomorphisms
f^{\ast}:E\xrightarrow{\cong}f^{\ast}E
and
f^{\ast}:E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\cong}f^{\ast}E^{\prime}
. We define the continuous homomorphism
\varpi^{\prime}:f^{\ast}E\to f^{\ast}E^{\prime}
by the formula

\forall\alpha\in E,\quad\varpi^{\prime}_{f^{\ast}\alpha}:=f^{\ast}\varpi_{\alpha}=\varpi_{\alpha}\times_{\bar{X}}s_{\#}^{\prime}

(where
s\in\Gamma_{k}
is the image of
\alpha
). Then
f^{\ast}\iota\circ f^{\ast}\alpha=(\iota\times_{\bar{X}}\operatorname{id}_{\bar{X}^{\prime}})\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{X}}s_{\#}^{\prime})=(\varpi_{\alpha}\times_{\bar{X}}s_{\#}^{\prime})\circ(\iota\times_{\bar{X}}\operatorname{id}_{\bar{X}^{\prime}})=\varpi^{\prime}_{f^{\ast}\alpha}\circ f^{\ast}\iota
. This means the pair
(f^{\ast}\iota,\varpi^{\prime}):(f^{\ast}\bar{Y},f^{\ast}E)\to(f^{\ast}\bar{Y}^{\prime},f^{\ast}E^{\prime})
is a morphism of descent data compatible with
\varphi
in the sense of Definition
2.1

(ii)
,
i.e.
,
f^{\ast}\lambda^{\prime}
is in relation
\varphi_{\ast}
with
f^{\ast}\lambda
.

2.4
Dévissage

Let
X
be a variety over a perfect field
k
. Given a torsor
Y\to X
under an algebraic
k
-group
G
and a normal Zariski closed subgroup
H\subseteq G
, we may put
Z:=Y/H
to obtain a torsor
Y\to Z
under
H
and a torsor
Z\to X
under
G/H
. A dévissage argument is something that allows one to deduce a property for the “total” torsor
Y\to X
from the same property for the above “intermediate” torsors. A difficulty in deploying such an argument is that when we have a
(G/H)
-torsor
Z^{\prime}\to X
of the same type as
Z
(by virtue of Proposition
2.4

(iii)
, this means
Z^{\prime}
is isomorphic to the twist
Z^{\tau}
of
Z
by a cocycle
\tau\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G/H)
), we do not necessarily have a torsor
Y^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime}
(under some
k
-form of
H
) such that the composite
Y^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime}\to X
is a torsor of the same type as
Y
. In fact, this is equivalent to the existence of a Galois cocycle
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G)
lifting
\tau
(and in the case where such a cocycle
\sigma
exists,
Y^{\prime}
is isomorphic to the twisted
X
-torsor
Y^{\sigma}
).

When torsors are replaced by descent types, the formalism for a dévissage argument turns out to be somewhat simpler. We shall study this problem in this paragraph by establishing some sort of “long exact sequence” associated with an exact sequence
1\to H\to G\to G/H\to 1
for the non-abelian
\mathrm{H}^{2}
set as well as for the set
\operatorname{DType}
.

Let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
and
L^{\prime}=(\bar{G}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
be
k
-kernels,
\varphi:L\to L^{\prime}
a surjective morphism, and
\bar{H}:=\operatorname{Ker}(\bar{G}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\bar{G}^{\prime})
. Then, the relation
\varphi_{\ast}:\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)\multimap\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime})
is a map. Let
E
(resp.
E^{\prime}
) be a (topological) extension of
\Gamma_{k}
by
\bar{G}(\bar{k})
(resp.
\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})
) such that
[E^{\prime}]=\varphi_{\ast}[E]
in
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime})
, that is, there is a continuous surjection
\phi:E\to E^{\prime}
fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1.}

Lemma 2.10
.

With the above notations, suppose that
E^{\prime}
has a continuous homomorphic section
\varsigma
(that is, the class
[E^{\prime}]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime})
is neutral). Then, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{H}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{\varsigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{G}(\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}

with exact rows, where
E_{\varsigma}:=\phi^{-1}(\varsigma(\Gamma_{k}))\subseteq E
. In particular, there exist a
k
-kernel
L_{\varsigma}=(\bar{H},\kappa_{\varsigma})
and a class
[E_{\varsigma}]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L_{\varsigma})
such that the inclusion
\bar{H}\hookrightarrow\bar{G}
induces a morphism
i:L_{\varsigma}\to L
of
k
-kernels, and
[E]
is in relation
i_{\ast}:\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L_{\varsigma})\multimap\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
with
[E_{\varsigma}]
.

Proof.

Let us show that the restriction
q|_{E_{\varsigma}}:E_{\varsigma}\to\Gamma_{k}
is surjective. Indeed, let
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and lift it to an element
\alpha\in E
. Since both
\phi(\alpha)\in E^{\prime}
and
\varsigma_{s}\in E^{\prime}
lift
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, there exists
g^{\prime}\in\bar{G}^{\prime}(\bar{k})
such that
\phi(\alpha)g^{\prime}=\varsigma_{s}
. Since
\varphi
is surjective,
g^{\prime}=\varphi(g)
for some
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
. Then
\phi(\alpha g)=\varsigma_{s}\in\varsigma(\Gamma_{k})
, or
\alpha g\in E_{\varsigma}
, which is an element satisfying
q(\alpha g)=s
.

It remains to check exactness at
E_{\varsigma}
, that is, to show that
\bar{H}(\bar{k})=\bar{G}(\bar{k})\cap E_{\varsigma}
inside
E
. Indeed, let
g\in\bar{G}(\bar{k})
such that
\varphi(g)\in\varsigma(\Gamma_{k})\subseteq E
. That
\varphi(g)\in E
is a lifting of
1\in\Gamma_{k}
forces
\varphi(g)=\varsigma_{1}=1
, or
g\in\bar{H}(\bar{k})
.

Let
X
be a quasi-projective, reduced, and geometrically connected variety over
k
. We recall from Lemma
2.7
that for each descent type
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
, the pushforward type
\varphi_{\ast}\lambda\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L^{\prime})
from Construction
2.6
is represented by a pair
(\bar{Z},E^{\prime})
, where
\bar{Z}:=\varphi_{\ast}\bar{Y}=\bar{Y}/\bar{H}
and
E^{\prime}:=\varphi_{\ast}E
. Indeed,
[E^{\prime}]=\varphi_{\ast}[E]
in
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L^{\prime})
.

Proposition 2.11
.

With the above notations, suppose that
Z\to X
is a torsor under a
k
-form
G^{\prime}
of
L^{\prime}
, of descent type
\mathbf{dtype}(Z)=\varphi_{\ast}\lambda
, which defines a continuous homomorphic section
\varsigma
of
E^{\prime}
(cf. Proposition
2.4

(i)
). Let
E_{Z}=:E_{\varsigma}\subseteq E
,
L_{Z}:=L_{\varsigma}=(\bar{H},\kappa_{\varsigma})
, and
i:L_{Z}\to L
be as in Lemma
2.10
.

(i).

We have
E_{Z}=E\cap\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/Z)
inside
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
. In particular,
\lambda_{Z}:=[\bar{Y},E_{Z}]
is a descent type on
Z
bound by
L_{Z}
.

(ii).

For every torsor
Y\to Z
under a
k
-form
H
of
L_{Z}
, of descent type
\lambda_{Z}
, the composite
Y\to Z\to X
is a torsor under a
k
-form
G
of
L
, of descent type
\lambda
. In particular, the inclusion
\bar{H}\hookrightarrow\bar{G}
is defined over
k
, and
G^{\prime}=G/H
.

Proof.

We show
(i)
. For each
\alpha\in E_{Z}
, one has
\varphi_{\ast}\alpha=\varsigma_{s}
for some
s\in\Gamma_{k}
. Hence, the diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma_{s}}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}}

commutes. Since
\varsigma_{s}\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/Z)
, we have
\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/Z)
. Conversely, if
\alpha\in E\cap\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/Z)
, then
\varphi_{\ast}\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Z}/Z)=\varsigma(\Gamma_{k})
by virtue of the exact sequence (
2.3
) (take
\bar{Y}=\bar{Z}
and
X=Z
). It follows that
\alpha\in E_{\varsigma}=E_{Z}
.

Let us now show
(ii)
. The torsor
Z\to X
defines a homomorphic section of
E_{Z}
,
a fortiori
a homomorphic section of
E
. In view of Proposition
2.4

(i)
, this section yields a
k
-form
G
of
L
, such that
\bar{H}\hookrightarrow\bar{G}
descends into a
k
-morphism
H\subseteq G
, and
G^{\prime}=G/H
. Furthermore, it gives rise to a torsor
Y\to X
under
G
such that
Y/H=Z
.

3
The abelian case

Let
X
be a non-empty variety over a perfect field
k
. In this §, we study the set
\operatorname{DType}(X,G):=\operatorname{DType}(X,\mathbf{lien}(G))
of descent type on
X
bound by
\mathbf{lien}(G)
, where
G
is a smooth commutative algebraic group over
k
. Our aim is to prove Theorem
A
.

3.1
External product

To define a group structure on abelian descent types in a conceptual way, we introduce the
external product
of descent types. First, we define this on the level of kernels. Let
L_{1}=(\bar{G}_{1},\kappa_{1})
and
L_{2}=(\bar{G}_{2},\kappa_{2})
be
k
-kernels. If
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and
\phi_{1}\in\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)
,
\phi_{2}\in\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{2}/k)
are
s
-semilinear automorphisms, then
\phi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\phi_{2}\in\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}((\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2})/k)
. Thus, we have a natural inclusion

\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{2}/k)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}((\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2})/k),

which induces a homomorphism

\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}_{2}/k)\to\operatorname{SOut}((\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2})/k).

Denote by
\kappa_{1}\boxtimes\kappa_{2}
the composite of this homomorphism with

\Gamma_{k}\xrightarrow{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})}\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}_{2}/k).

Then
L_{1}\times L_{2}:=(\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2},\kappa_{1}\boxtimes\kappa_{2})
is a
k
-kernel. If
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are respective
k
-forms of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
(that is,
L_{1}=\mathbf{lien}(G_{1})
and
L_{2}=\mathbf{lien}(G_{2})
), then
G_{1}\times_{k}G_{2}
is a
k
-form of
L_{1}\times L_{2}
.

It is immediate to see that the operation “
\times
” on
k
-kernels is associative. Namely, if
L_{3}=(\bar{G}_{3},\kappa_{3})
is a third
k
-kernel, then the canonical isomorphism
(\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2})\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{3}\cong\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}(\bar{G}_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{3})
of algebraic
\bar{k}
-groups induces an isomorphism
(L_{1}\times L_{2})\times L_{3}\cong L_{1}\times(L_{2}\times L_{3})
of
k
-kernels. In fact, they are both isomorphic to the
k
-kernel
L_{1}\times L_{2}\times L_{3}=(\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{3},\kappa_{1}\boxtimes\kappa_{2}\boxtimes\kappa_{3})
, where
\kappa_{1}\boxtimes\kappa_{2}\boxtimes\kappa_{3}
is the composite

\Gamma_{k}\xrightarrow{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3})}\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}_{2}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SOut}(\bar{G}_{3}/k)\to\operatorname{SOut}((\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{3})/k),

the second arrow being induced by the natural inclusion

\displaystyle\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{2}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{3}/k)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}((\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{3})/k),

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto\phi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\phi_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\phi_{3}.

In what follows, we abusively identify
(L_{1}\times L_{2})\times L_{3}
and
L_{1}\times(L_{2}\times L_{3})
to
L_{1}\times L_{2}\times L_{3}
. We also note that the neutral element for the operation “
\times
” is the
trivial

k
-kernel
L_{\operatorname{triv}}:=\mathbf{lien}(\{1\})
.

Let
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
be non-empty
k
-varieties. If
\bar{Y}_{1}
and
\bar{Y}_{2}
are respective non-empty
\bar{k}
-varieties equipped with morphisms
\bar{Y}_{1}\to\bar{X}_{1}
and
\bar{Y}_{2}\to\bar{X}_{2}
, we have a natural inclusion

\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}_{1}/X_{1})\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}_{2}/X_{2})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{SAut}((\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2})/(X_{1}\times_{k}X_{2})),\quad(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\mapsto\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}.

(3.1)

Indeed, if
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and if
\alpha_{1}\in\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}_{1}/X_{1})
and
\alpha_{2}\in\operatorname{Aut}(\bar{Y}_{2}/X_{2})
are
s
-semilinear, then the automorphism
\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}\in\operatorname{Aut}((\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2})/(X_{1}\times_{k}X_{2}))
is
s
-semilinear. Now, let
\lambda_{1}=[\bar{Y}_{1},E_{1}]
(resp.
\lambda_{2}=[\bar{Y}_{2},E_{2}]
) be a descent type on
X_{1}
(resp.
X_{2}
) bound by a
k
-kernel
L_{1}=(\bar{G}_{1},\kappa_{1})
(resp.
L_{2}=(\bar{G}_{2},\kappa_{2})
). This means in particular that
E_{1}
(resp.
E_{2}
) is a topological extension of
\Gamma_{k}
by
\bar{G}_{1}(\bar{k})
(resp.
\bar{G}_{2}(\bar{k})
) bound by the
k
-kernel
L_{1}
(resp.
L_{2}
). One checks with ease that the extension

1\to\bar{G}_{1}(\bar{k})\times\bar{G}_{2}(\bar{k})\to E_{1}\times_{\Gamma_{k}}E_{2}\to\Gamma_{k}\to 1

is bound by the product kernel
L_{1}\times L_{2}
. Let us denote by
E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2}
the image of
E_{1}\times_{\Gamma_{k}}E_{2}
by the inclusion (
3.1
). Then
[\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2},E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2}]
is a descent type on
X_{1}\times_{k}X_{2}
bound by
L_{1}\times L_{2}
.

Definition 3.1
.

We call
\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2}:=[\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2},E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X_{1}\times_{k}X_{2},L_{1}\times L_{2})
the
external product
of
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

(i).

For
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
X_{i}
be a non-empty
k
-variety,
L_{i}=(\bar{G}_{i},\kappa_{i})
a
k
-kernel, and
\lambda_{i}=[\bar{Y}_{i},E_{i}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X_{i},L_{i})
. Under the identification
(L_{1}\times L_{2})\times L_{3}=L_{1}\times(L_{2}\times L_{3})=L_{1}\times L_{2}\times L_{3}
, we have
(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})\times\lambda_{3}=\lambda_{1}\times(\lambda_{2}\times\lambda_{3})
in
\operatorname{DType}(X_{1}\times_{k}X_{2}\times_{k}X_{3},L_{1}\times L_{2}\times L_{3})
.

(ii).

The set
\operatorname{DType}(\operatorname{Spec}(k),L_{\operatorname{triv}})
has a unique element
\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}
. Furthermore, let
X
be a non-empty
k
-variety,
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
a
k
-kernel, and
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
. Under the identifications
L_{\operatorname{triv}}\times L=L\times L_{\operatorname{triv}}=L
, one has
\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}\times\lambda=\lambda\times\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}=\lambda
.

Proof.

(i).

We consider the
(\bar{X}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X}_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X}_{3})
-isomorphism

\iota:(\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2})\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{3}\xrightarrow{\cong}\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}(\bar{Y}_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{3}),\quad((y_{1},y_{2}),y_{3})\mapsto(y_{1},(y_{2},y_{3}))

of torsors under
\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{3}
. An element of
(E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2})\boxtimes E_{3}
has the form
(\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2})\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{3}
, where
(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})\in E_{1}\times_{\Gamma_{k}}E_{2}\times_{\Gamma_{k}}E_{3}
. Then, we have

\iota\circ((\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2})\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{3})\circ\iota^{-1}=\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}(\alpha_{2}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{3})\in E_{1}\boxtimes(E_{2}\boxtimes E_{3}).

It follows from definition that
(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})\times\lambda_{3}=\lambda_{1}\times(\lambda_{2}\times\lambda_{3})
.

(ii).

Since
\operatorname{Aut}(\operatorname{Spec}(\bar{k})/\operatorname{Spec}(k))=\{\operatorname{Spec}(s^{-1}):s\in\Gamma_{k}\}:=E_{\operatorname{triv}}
, it is immediate that the only element of
\operatorname{DType}(\operatorname{Spec}(k),L_{\operatorname{triv}})
is
\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}=(\operatorname{Spec}(\bar{k}),E_{\operatorname{triv}})
. For
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
, the first projection
\pi:\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\to\bar{Y}
is an
\bar{X}
-isomorphism of torsors under
\bar{G}
. Furthermore, for any
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and any
s
-semilinear automorphism
\alpha\in E
, one has
\pi\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}\operatorname{Spec}(s^{-1}))\circ\pi^{-1}=\alpha\in E
. It follows that
\lambda\times\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}=\lambda
. Similarly,
\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}\times\lambda=\lambda
.

Let us now show that the external product behaves in a functorial way.

Proposition 3.3
.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
f_{i}:X_{i}^{\prime}\to X_{i}
be a morphism between non-empty
k
-varieties,
\varphi_{i}:L_{i}\to L_{i}^{\prime}
a morphism of
k
-kernels, and
\lambda_{i}=[\bar{Y}_{i},E_{i}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X_{i},L_{i})
.

(i).

We have
(f_{1}\times_{k}f_{2})^{\ast}(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})=f_{1}^{\ast}\lambda_{1}\times f_{2}^{\ast}\lambda_{2}
in
\operatorname{DType}(X_{1}^{\prime}\times_{k}X_{2}^{\prime},L_{1}\times L_{2})
.

(ii).

The underlying morphism
\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2}
of algebraic
\bar{k}
-groups is a morphism
L_{1}\times L_{2}\to L_{1}^{\prime}\times L_{2}^{\prime}
of
k
-kernels. For
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
\lambda_{i}^{\prime}=[\bar{Y}_{i}^{\prime},E_{i}^{\prime}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X_{i},L_{i}^{\prime})
be a descent type which is in relation
\varphi_{i\ast}
with
\lambda_{i}
. Then,
\lambda_{1}^{\prime}\times\lambda_{2}^{\prime}
is in relation
(\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2})_{\ast}
with
\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2}
. In particular, if either the morphisms
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
are surjective or the underlying
\bar{k}
-groups of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are commutative, then

(\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2})_{\ast}(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})=\varphi_{1\ast}\lambda_{1}\times\varphi_{2\ast}\lambda_{2}\in\operatorname{DType}(X_{1}\times_{k}X_{2},L_{1}^{\prime}\times L_{2}^{\prime}).

(iii).

If
\lambda_{i}=\mathbf{dtype}(Y_{i})
for some torsor
Y_{i}\to X_{i}
under a
k
-form
G_{i}
of
L_{i}
,
i=1,2
, then
\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2}=\mathbf{dtype}(Y_{1}\times_{k}Y_{2})
in
\operatorname{DType}(L_{1}\times L_{2})
.

Proof.

(i).

Let
X:=X_{1}\times_{k}X_{2}
,
X^{\prime}:=X_{1}^{\prime}\times_{k}X_{2}^{\prime}
,
\bar{Y}:=\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2}
,
\bar{Y}_{1}^{\prime}:=\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{X}_{1}}\bar{X}^{\prime}_{1}
, and
\bar{Y}_{2}^{\prime}:=\bar{Y}_{2}\times_{\bar{X}_{2}}\bar{X}^{\prime}_{2}
. By definition, we have
(f_{1}\times_{k}f_{2})^{\ast}(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})=[\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{X}}\bar{X}^{\prime},(f_{1}\times_{k}f_{2})^{\ast}(E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2})]
and
f_{1}^{\ast}\lambda_{1}\times f_{2}^{\ast}\lambda_{2}=[\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{2},f_{1}^{\ast}E_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{\ast}E_{2}]
. Consider the isomorphism of
\bar{X}^{\prime}
-torsors

\iota:\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{X}}\bar{X}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\cong}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{2},\quad((y_{1},y_{2}),(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}))\mapsto((y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}),(y_{2},x_{2}^{\prime})).

An element
\alpha\in(f_{1}\times_{k}f_{2})^{\ast}(E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2})
has the form
\alpha=(f_{1}\times_{k}f_{2})^{\ast}(\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2})
, where
\alpha_{i}\in E_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, and they have the same image in
\Gamma_{k}
(let us denote it by
s
). For all
y_{1}^{\prime}\in\bar{Y}_{1}^{\prime}
and
y_{2}^{\prime}\in\bar{Y}_{2}^{\prime}
, write them as
y_{1}^{\prime}=(y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})
and
y_{2}^{\prime}=(y_{2},x_{2}^{\prime})
(where
y_{1}\in\bar{Y}_{1}
,
x_{1}^{\prime}\in\bar{X}_{1}^{\prime}
,
y_{2}\in\bar{Y}_{2}
, and
x_{2}^{\prime}\in\bar{X}_{2}^{\prime}
). Then, we have

\displaystyle(\iota\circ\alpha\circ\iota^{-1})(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(\iota\circ\alpha)((y_{1},y_{2}),(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\iota((\alpha_{1}(y_{1}),\alpha_{2}(y_{2})),(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-6.9427pt{s}\kern 4.53473pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-6.9427pt{s}\kern \displaystyle=((\alpha_{1}(y_{1}),\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-6.9427pt{s}\kern 2.00034pt}}}{}_{1}^{\prime}),(\alpha_{2}(y_{2}),\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-6.9427pt{s}\kern
