4.53473pt}}_{{\kern-11.99579pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.53473pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-4.55222pt{s}\kern 2.80048pt}}_{{\kern-8.16158pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.80048pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}x^{{\kern-3.75208pt{s}\kern 2.00034pt}}_{{\kern-7.36143pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.00034pt}}}{}_{2}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=((f_{1}^{\ast}\alpha_{1})(y_{1}^{\prime}),(f_{2}^{\ast}\alpha_{2})(y_{2}^{\prime})),

or
\iota\circ\alpha\circ\iota^{-1}=f_{1}^{\ast}\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}f_{2}^{\ast}\alpha_{2}\in f_{1}^{\ast}E_{1}\boxtimes f_{2}^{\ast}E_{2}
. It follows that the isomorphism
\iota
identifies the descent types
(f_{1}\times_{k}f_{2})^{\ast}(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})
and
f_{1}^{\ast}\lambda_{1}\times f_{2}^{\ast}\lambda_{2}
.

(ii).

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, let
L_{i}=(\bar{G}_{i},\kappa_{i})
and
L_{i}^{\prime}=(\bar{G}^{\prime}_{i},\kappa_{i}^{\prime})
. That
\varphi_{i}
is a morphism of
k
-kernels means there are respective continuous set-theoretic liftings
\phi_{i}:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{i}/k)
and
\phi_{i}^{\prime}:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}^{\prime}_{i}/k)
of
\kappa_{i}
and
\kappa_{i}^{\prime}
such that
\varphi_{i}\circ\phi_{i}(s)=\phi^{\prime}_{i}(s)\circ\varphi_{i}
for all
s\in\Gamma_{k}
. Let
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
denote the respective composites

\Gamma_{k}\xrightarrow{(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{2}/k)\xrightarrow{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\mapsto\psi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\psi_{2}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}((\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2})/k)

and

\Gamma_{k}\xrightarrow{(\phi_{1}^{\prime},\phi_{2}^{\prime})}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{1}^{\prime}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{2}^{\prime}/k)\xrightarrow{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\mapsto\psi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\psi_{2}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}((\bar{G}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}^{\prime}_{2})/k)

Then
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
are respective continuous set-theoretic liftings of
\kappa_{1}\boxtimes\kappa_{2}
and
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}\boxtimes\kappa_{2}^{\prime}
. Furthermore,
(\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2})\circ\phi(s)=\phi^{\prime}(s)\circ(\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2})
for all
s\in\Gamma_{k}
. Hence,
\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2}
is indeed a morphism
L_{1}\times L_{2}\to L_{1}^{\prime}\times L_{2}^{\prime}
of
k
-kernels.

Now, since
\lambda_{i}^{\prime}
is in relation
\varphi_{i\ast}
with
\lambda_{i}
, there exist an
\varphi_{i}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-morphism
\iota_{i}:\bar{Y}_{i}\to\bar{Y}_{i}^{\prime}
and a continuous homomorphism
\varpi_{i}:E_{i}\to E_{i}^{\prime}
satisfying the conditions in Definition
2.1

(ii)
. Let
\iota:=\iota_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\iota_{2}
, and let
\varpi:E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2}\to E_{1}^{\prime}\boxtimes E_{2}^{\prime}
be the continuous homomorphism
\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}\mapsto\varpi_{1}(\alpha_{1})\times_{\bar{k}}\varpi_{2}(\alpha_{2})
. Then the pair
(\iota,\varpi)
defines a morphism of descent data
(\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2},E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2})\to(\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}^{\prime}_{2},E^{\prime}_{1}\boxtimes E^{\prime}_{2})
compatible with
\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2}
,
i.e.
,
\lambda_{1}^{\prime}\times\lambda_{2}^{\prime}
is in relation
(\varphi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\varphi_{2})_{\ast}
with
\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2}
.

(iii).

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, the
k
-form
G_{i}
defines a continuous homomorphic lifting
\phi_{i}:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{i}/k)
of
\kappa_{i}
, and we have
\mathbf{dtype}(Y_{i})=[\bar{Y}_{i},E_{i}]
, where
E_{i}:=\{\phi_{i}(s)\circ\rho_{g_{i}}:s\in\Gamma_{k},g_{i}\in G_{i}(\bar{k})\}
. The semilinear action
\phi
of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{G}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}_{2}

via
its
k
-form
G_{1}\times_{k}G_{2}
is given by the composite

\Gamma_{k}\xrightarrow{(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{1}/k)\times_{\Gamma_{k}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}(\bar{G}_{2}/k)\xrightarrow{(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})\mapsto\psi_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\psi_{2}}\operatorname{SAut}^{\operatorname{gr}}((\bar{G}_{1}\times\bar{G}_{2})/k),

and we have
\mathbf{dtype}(Y_{1}\times_{k}Y_{2})=[\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2},E]
, where
E:=\{\phi(s)\circ\rho_{g}:s\in\Gamma_{k},g\in G_{1}(\bar{k})\times G_{2}(\bar{k})\}
. For all
s\in\Gamma_{k}
,
g_{1}\in G_{1}(\bar{k})
, and
g_{2}\in G_{2}(\bar{k})
, we have

(\phi_{1}(s)\circ\rho_{g_{1}})\times_{\bar{k}}(\phi_{2}(s)\circ\rho_{g_{2}})=\phi_{s}\circ\rho_{(g_{1},g_{2})}.

It follows that
E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2}=E
, or
\mathbf{dtype}(Y_{1})\times\mathbf{dtype}(Y_{2})=\mathbf{dtype}(Y_{1}\times_{k}Y_{2})
.

3.2
The group law

Let
X
be a non-empty variety over a perfect field
k
, and
G
a smooth commutative algebraic
k
-group. By commutativity, the multiplication morphism
\nabla:G\times_{k}G\to G
is a morphism of algebraic
k
-groups. Let
\Delta:X\to X\times_{k}X
denote the diagonal morphism.

Definition 3.4
.

Let
\lambda
and
\lambda^{\prime}
be descent types on
X
bound by
\mathbf{lien}(G)
. We define their
sum

\lambda+\lambda^{\prime}\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
to be the descent type
\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda\times\lambda^{\prime})=\nabla_{\ast}\Delta^{\ast}(\lambda\times\lambda^{\prime})
(
cf.
Definition
3.1
and Lemma
2.9
).

Proposition 3.5
.

The operation “
+
” from Definition
3.4
makes
\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
an abelian group, functorial in
X
and
G
. More precisely, we have the following claims.

(i).

The neutral element is the descent type of the trivial torsor
G_{X}=G\times_{k}X\to X
.

(ii).

The inverse of a descent type
\lambda
is
-\lambda=i_{\ast}\lambda
, where
i:G\to G
is the inversion morphism.

Proof.

First, we show associativity. Indeed, we have the standard properties

(\Delta\times_{k}\operatorname{id}_{X})\circ\Delta=(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}\Delta)\circ\Delta\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla\circ(\nabla\times_{k}\operatorname{id}_{G})=\nabla\circ(\operatorname{id}_{G}\times_{k}\nabla).

(3.2)

Let
\lambda
,
\lambda^{\prime}
,
\lambda^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
, then

\displaystyle(\lambda+\lambda^{\prime})+\lambda^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda\times\lambda^{\prime})\times\lambda^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\text{by Definition \ref{defn:SumOfDescentType}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\Delta\times_{k}\operatorname{id}_{X})^{\ast}(\nabla\times_{k}\operatorname{id}_{G})_{\ast}((\lambda\times\lambda^{\prime})\times\lambda^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:ExternalProduct}\ref{prop:ExternalProduct1} and \ref{prop:ExternalProduct2}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}(\Delta\times_{k}\operatorname{id}_{X})^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\nabla\times_{k}\operatorname{id}_{G})_{\ast}(\lambda\times(\lambda^{\prime}\times\lambda^{\prime\prime})),

\displaystyle\text{by Lemmata \ref{lemm:DescentTypePullbackVsPushforward} and \ref{lemm:ExternalProduct} \ref{lemm:ExternalProduct1}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}\Delta)^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{G}\times_{k}\nabla)_{\ast}(\lambda\times(\lambda^{\prime}\times\lambda^{\prime\prime})),

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:SumOfDescentType1}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}\Delta)^{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{G}\times_{k}\nabla)_{\ast}(\lambda\times(\lambda^{\prime}\times\lambda^{\prime\prime})),

\displaystyle\text{by Lemma \ref{lemm:DescentTypePullbackVsPushforward}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda\times\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda^{\prime}\times\lambda^{\prime\prime})),

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:ExternalProduct}\ref{prop:ExternalProduct1} and \ref{prop:ExternalProduct2}},

\displaystyle=\lambda+(\lambda^{\prime}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle\text{by Definition \ref{defn:SumOfDescentType}}.

Next, we show commutativity. Let
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]
and
\lambda^{\prime}=[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]
. Let
\iota:\bar{Y}\times\bar{Y}^{\prime}\to\bar{Y}^{\prime}\times\bar{Y}
be the isomorphism
(y,y^{\prime})\mapsto(y^{\prime},y)
. An element of
E\boxtimes E^{\prime}
has the form
\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha^{\prime}
, where
(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})\in E\times_{\Gamma_{k}}E^{\prime}
. It is clear that
\iota\circ(\alpha\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha^{\prime})\circ\iota^{-1}=\alpha^{\prime}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha\in E^{\prime}\boxtimes E
, thus
\iota
identifies
\lambda\times\lambda^{\prime}=[\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}^{\prime},E\boxtimes E^{\prime}]
to
\lambda^{\prime}\times\lambda=[\bar{Y}^{\prime}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y},E^{\prime}\boxtimes E]
. It follows that

\lambda+\lambda^{\prime}=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda\times\lambda^{\prime})=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda^{\prime}\times\lambda)=\lambda^{\prime}+\lambda.

(i).

We construct the neutral element for “
+
”. Let
p:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
(resp.
e:\{1\}\to G
) denote the structure morphism (resp. the inclusion of the neutral element. We have the standard properties

(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}p)\circ\Delta=\operatorname{id}_{X}\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla\circ(\operatorname{id}_{G}\times_{k}e)=\operatorname{id}_{G}.

(3.3)

Viewing
G
as the trivial
k
-torsor under itself, we have
G_{X}=p^{\ast}G
and
G=e_{\ast}\{1\}
. Hence

\mathbf{dtype}(G_{X})=p^{\ast}\mathbf{dtype}(G)=p^{\ast}e_{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}.

(3.4)

where
\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}=\mathbf{dtype}(\{1\})
is the unique element of
\operatorname{DType}(\operatorname{Spec}(k),\{1\})
(
cf.
Lemma
3.2

(ii)
). Now, for any
\lambda\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda+\mathbf{dtype}(G_{X})

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda\times G_{X}),

\displaystyle\text{by Definition \ref{defn:SumOfDescentType}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda\times p^{\ast}e_{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}),

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:SumOfDescentType3}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}p)^{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}e)_{\ast}(\lambda\times\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}),

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:ExternalProduct} \ref{prop:ExternalProduct1} and \ref{prop:ExternalProduct2}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}p)^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}e)_{\ast}\lambda,

\displaystyle\text{by Lemmata \ref{lemm:DescentTypePullbackVsPushforward} and \ref{lemm:ExternalProduct} \ref{lemm:ExternalProduct2}},

\displaystyle=\operatorname{id}_{X}^{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{G})_{\ast}\lambda,

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:SumOfDescentType2}},

\displaystyle=\lambda,

showing that
\mathbf{dtype}(G_{X})
is the neutral element for the operation “
+
” on
\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
.

(ii).

Finally, we construct the inverse (for “
+
”) of a given element
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
. Since
G
is commutative, the inversion
i:G\to G
is a morphism of algebraic
k
-groups. Let
\pi:G\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
denote the structure morphism. Then, we have the standard properties

\nabla\circ(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}i)=e\circ(\pi\times_{k}\pi)\quad\text{and}\quad(p\times_{k}p)\circ\Delta=p.

(3.5)

We observe that
p^{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}=[\bar{X},\{s_{\#}:s\in\Gamma_{k}\}]
by Construction
2.5
. The structure morphism
f:\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
can be seen as a
\pi
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-morphism. We have a continuous homomorphism

\varpi:E\to\{s_{\#}:s\in\Gamma_{k}\},\quad\alpha\mapsto q(\alpha)_{\#},

where
q:E\to\Gamma_{k}
is the restriction of the natural homomorphism
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)\to\Gamma_{k}
. For any
\alpha\in E
, one has
f\circ\alpha=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ f
. In other words, the pair
(f,\varpi):\lambda\to p^{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}
is a morphism of descent data
(\bar{Y},E)\to(\bar{X},\{s_{\#}:s\in\Gamma_{k}\})
compatible with
\pi
in the sense of Definition
2.1

(ii)
. It follows that

\pi_{\ast}\lambda=p^{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}.

(3.6)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\lambda+i_{\ast}\lambda

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\lambda\times i_{\ast}\lambda),

\displaystyle\text{by Definition \ref{defn:SumOfDescentType}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\operatorname{id}_{X}\times_{k}i)_{\ast}(\lambda\times\lambda),

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:ExternalProduct} \ref{prop:ExternalProduct2}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}e_{\ast}(\pi\times_{k}\pi)_{\ast}(\lambda\times\lambda),

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:SumOfDescentType4}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}e_{\ast}(\pi_{\ast}\lambda\times\pi_{\ast}\lambda),

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:ExternalProduct} \ref{prop:ExternalProduct2}},

\displaystyle=e_{\ast}\Delta^{\ast}(p^{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}\circ p^{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}}),

\displaystyle\text{by Lemma \ref{lemm:DescentTypePullbackVsPushforward} and \eqref{eq:SumOfDescentType5}},

\displaystyle=e_{\ast}\Delta^{\ast}(p\times_{k}p)^{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}},

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:ExternalProduct} \ref{prop:ExternalProduct1}},

\displaystyle=e_{\ast}p^{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}},

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:SumOfDescentType4}},

\displaystyle=p^{\ast}e_{\ast}\lambda_{\operatorname{triv}},

\displaystyle\text{by Lemma \ref{lemm:DescentTypePullbackVsPushforward}},

\displaystyle=\mathbf{dtype}(G_{X}),

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:SumOfDescentType3}},

showing that
i_{\ast}\lambda
is the inverse of
\lambda
for the operation “
+
”.

Functoriality and
X
and
G
follows immediately from Lemma
2.9
and Definition
3.4
.

Recall that a descent type
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
yields a class
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
as well as a class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
(
cf.
Remarks
2.2

(i)
).

Lemma 3.6
.

The maps

\mathbf{dtype}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\to\operatorname{DType}(X,G),

\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})),\quad[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[\bar{Y}],

and

\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G),\quad[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[E]

are group homomorphisms.

Proof.

If
Y\to X
and
Y^{\prime}\to X
are torsors under
G
, then
Y\times_{k}Y^{\prime}\to X\times_{k}X
is a torsor under
G\times_{k}G
, and
[Y]+[Y^{\prime}]=[\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(Y\times_{k}Y^{\prime})]
in
\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)
. On the other hand, one has

\displaystyle\mathbf{dtype}(\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(Y\times_{k}Y^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}\mathbf{dtype}(Y\times_{k}Y^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\text{by Constructions \ref{cons:PullbackOfDescentType} and \ref{cons:PushforwardOfDescentType}},

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\mathbf{dtype}(Y)\times\mathbf{dtype}(Y^{\prime})),

\displaystyle\text{by Proposition \ref{prop:ExternalProduct} \ref{prop:ExternalProduct3}},

\displaystyle=\mathbf{dtype}(Y)+\mathbf{dtype}(Y^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\text{by Definition \ref{defn:SumOfDescentType}}.

This shows that
\mathbf{dtype}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\to\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
is a group homomorphism.

Let
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]
and
\lambda^{\prime}=[\bar{Y}^{\prime},E^{\prime}]
be elements of
\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
. Then

\lambda+\lambda^{\prime}=[\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}^{\prime})],\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(E\boxtimes E^{\prime})]

by Constructions
2.5
and
2.6
, Lemma
2.8
, and Definition
3.4
. First, we have

\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}^{\prime})]=[\bar{Y}]+[\bar{Y}^{\prime}]

in
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})
, which means the map

\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})),\quad[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[\bar{Y}],

is a group homomorphism. Second, by the description of the addition in
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
in terms of group extensions (the “Baer sum”), we have

[E]+[E^{\prime}]=[\nabla_{\ast}(E\times_{\Gamma_{k}}E^{\prime})]=[\nabla_{\ast}(E\boxtimes E^{\prime})]=[\Delta^{\ast}\nabla_{\ast}(E\boxtimes E^{\prime})].

This shows that the map

\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G),\quad[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[E],

is also a group homomorphism.

3.3
The fundamental exact sequence

In this paragraph, we establish Theorem
3.7
. Keep the notations
k
,
X
, and
G
from the previous paragraph, and let
p:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
denote the structure morphism.

Theorem 3.7
(Theorem
A
).

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{dtype}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[E]}
\scriptstyle{[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[\bar{Y}]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{trans}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G)}

(3.7)

where the bottom row is the exact sequence issued from the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence

\mathrm{H}^{p}(k,\mathrm{H}^{q}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(X,G)

(in particular,
\mathbf{trans}
stands for the “transgression” map). The top row of (
3.7
) is a complex, which is exact whenever
X
is quasi-projective, reduced, and geometrically connected.

We consider the four squares of (
3.7
) from left to right. The first and the last squares obviously commute. The second square commutes by the very definition of the map
\mathbf{dtype}
. As for the third square, one requires a further investigation of the transgression map
\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))
. We recall that the action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
G(\bar{X})
is defined as follows. For any
\bar{k}
-morphism
\sigma:\bar{X}\to\bar{G}
and any
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, the
\bar{k}
-morphism
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 4.63394pt}}_{{\kern-12.095pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.63394pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 4.63394pt}}_{{\kern-12.095pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.63394pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-4.55167pt{s}\kern 2.79993pt}}_{{\kern-8.16103pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.79993pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-3.75168pt{s}\kern 1.99994pt}}_{{\kern-7.36104pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.99994pt}}}:\bar{X}\to\bar{G}
is given by the formula

\forall x\in\bar{X},\quad(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 4.63394pt}}_{{\kern-12.095pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.63394pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-7.04192pt{s}\kern 2.00034pt}}}).

(3.8)

The stabilizer of any
\sigma\in G(\bar{X})
is open, since such a
\bar{k}
-morphism is defined over some finite extension of
k
. Again, let
f:\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
be a torsor under
\bar{G}
. Denote by
\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
the subgroup of
\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)
consisting of semilinear

-automorphisms
\alpha
such that
\alpha\circ\rho_{g}=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-10.55373pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-25.1819pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-10.55373pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 3.68231pt}}_{{\kern-25.1819pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.68231pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.76657pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 1.79495pt}}_{{\kern-17.21523pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.79495pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}g^{{\kern-6.25372pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 1.2821pt}}_{{\kern-16.70238pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.45728pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74939pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.2821pt}}}}\circ\alpha
for all
g\in G(\bar{k})
(where
q:\operatorname{SAut}(\bar{Y}/X)\to\Gamma_{k}
is the natural continuous and open map from (
2.3
)). This is equivalent to saying that
\alpha
induces a
\bar{G}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-isomorphism
\phi_{q(\alpha)}\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}
, where

is the semilinear Galois action on

associated with its
k
-form
G
(
cf.
Remarks
2.2

(i)
). We have
\operatorname{Ker}(q)\cap\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)=\operatorname{Aut}_{\bar{G}}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X})
, the subgroup of
\bar{G}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-automorphism of

, which, by
[

, is

.
We obtain a sequence of topological groups

1\to G(\bar{X})\xrightarrow{\sigma\mapsto\rho_{\sigma}^{-1}}\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)\xrightarrow{q}\Gamma_{k}\to 1,

(3.9)

which is exact except possibly at the last non-trivial term.

Proposition 3.8
.

With the above notations, the following claims hold.

(i).

The continuous homomorphism
q
from (
3.9
) is open.

(ii).

We have
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
if and only if (
3.9
) is exact, i.e., if
q
is surjective.

(iii).

When (
3.9
) is exact, the action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
G(\bar{X})
induced by conjugation in
\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
is precisely the one defined in (
3.8
).

(iv).

Suppose that
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
. Then the transgression map

\mathbf{trans}:\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))

(issued from the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence
\mathrm{H}^{p}(k,\mathrm{H}^{q}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(X,G)
) takes
[\bar{Y}]
to the class of the extension (
3.9
) (cf.
[
Gir71
, Chapitre VIII, §8]
).

(ii).

That

(iii).

It follows that
\alpha\circ\rho_{\sigma}\circ\alpha^{-1}=\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-5.9387pt{s}\kern 4.07585pt}}_{{\kern-9.54807pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.07585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-5.9387pt{s}\kern 4.07585pt}}_{{\kern-9.54807pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.07585pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-3.39404pt{s}\kern 1.99994pt}}_{{\kern-5.97217pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.99994pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\sigma^{{\kern-2.82263pt{s}\kern 1.42853pt}}_{{\kern-5.40076pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.42853pt}}}}
, which is exactly what need to be proved.

(iv).

First, we note that the extension (
3.9
) has a continuous set-theoretic section. Indeed, let
K/k
be a finite extension such that there exists a torsor
Y\to X_{K}
under
G_{K}
whose base change to
\bar{k}
is isomorphic to
\bar{Y}
. The action of
\Gamma_{K}
on
Y
yields a continuous homomorphism
\varsigma:\Gamma_{K}\to\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
such that
q\circ\varsigma=\operatorname{id}_{\Gamma_{K}}
. For any
\gamma\in\operatorname{Gal}(K/k)=\Gamma_{k}/\Gamma_{K}
, choose an arbitrary lifting
s_{\gamma}\in\Gamma_{k}
, and lift it to an arbitrary element
\alpha_{\gamma}\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
(which is possible by virtue of
(ii)
). Then, each element
s\in\Gamma_{k}
can be uniquely written as
s=s_{\gamma}t
for some
\gamma\in\operatorname{Gal}(K/k)
and
t\in\Gamma_{K}
, and the map

\Gamma_{K}\to\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X),\quad s_{\gamma}t\mapsto\alpha_{\gamma}\varsigma_{t}

is a continuous set-theoretic section of
q
.

Recall the description of
\mathbf{trans}([\bar{Y}])
in terms of
k
-
gerbes
(see
e.g.

[
DLA19
, pp. 10–11]
). Let
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}}
be the “
k
-gerbe of forms of
\bar{Y}
”, that is, for every finite extension
K/k
, the fibre category
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}}(K)
is the groupoid of
K
-form of
\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
. This is not necessarily an
algebraic

k
-gerbe; it is bound by the sheaf
p_{\ast}p^{\ast}G
(where
p:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
is the structure morphism), or equivalently, the
\Gamma_{k}
-module
G(\bar{X})
. Nevertheless, one can speak of its class
[\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}}]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))
, which turns out to be exactly
\mathbf{trans}([\bar{Y}])
(see
[
Gir71
, Chapitre V, §3.1.6 and §3.2.1]
). To translate the description of
[\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}}]
into one in terms of cocycles, we refer to the general result in
[
Bre94
, §2]
(if
p_{\ast}p^{\ast}G
is representable by a smooth algebraic group over
k
,
e.g.
, when
p_{\ast}p^{\ast}G=G
, this can be found in
[
DLA19
, p. 13]
). The result is as follows. Take any continuous set-theoretic section
\alpha:\Gamma_{k}\to\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
, and put
\sigma_{s,t}:=\alpha_{st}\circ\alpha_{t}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{s}^{-1}\in G(\bar{X})
for all
s,t\in\Gamma_{k}
. Then, the continuous
2
-cocycle
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))
represents
[\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}}]
. But, by definition, it also represents the class of the extension (
3.9
).

To give a descent type
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
is to give a torsor
f:\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
under
\bar{G}
such that
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
and a subgroup
E\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
extension of
\Gamma_{k}
of
G(\bar{k})
. In particular, by virtue of Proposition
3.8

(iv)
, the natural map
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))
(induced by the inclusion
G(\bar{k})\hookrightarrow G(\bar{X})
) takes
[E]
to
\mathbf{trans}([\bar{Y}])
. This show that the third square in (
3.7
) commutes. It remains to investigate its top row, in order to finish the proof of Theorem
3.7
.

End of proof of Theorem
3.7
.

If
Z
is any
k
-torsor under
G
, then Proposition
2.4

(iii)
(applied to
X=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
) tells us that
\mathbf{dtype}(Z)=\mathbf{dtype}(G)=0
in
\operatorname{DType}(\operatorname{Spec}(k),G)
. By functoriality, we have
\mathbf{dtype}(p^{\ast}Z)=p^{\ast}\mathbf{dtype}(Z)=0
in
\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
, that is, the sequence

\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}}\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{dtype}}\operatorname{DType}(X,G)

is a complex. Next, if
Y\to X
is a torsor under
G
, then
\mathbf{dtype}(Y)=[\bar{Y},E_{Y}]
and
[E_{Y}]=0
in
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
by definition of the map
\mathbf{dtype}
. Thus, the sequence

\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\xrightarrow{\mathbf{dtype}}\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\xrightarrow{[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[E]}\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)

is a complex. Finally, the sequence

\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\xrightarrow{[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[E]}\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\xrightarrow{p^{\ast}}\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G)

is a complex because the bottom row of (
3.7
) is a complex.

Now, we assume that
X
is quasi-projective, reduced, and geometrically connected. We shall show that this implies the exactness of the top row of (
3.7
). Exactness at the term
\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)
follows from Proposition
2.4

(iii)
, while exactness at
\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
is a consequence of Proposition
2.4

(i)
. Now, let
\eta\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
be a class represented by a topological extension
E
of
\Gamma_{k}
by
G(\bar{k})
, such that
p^{\ast}\eta=0
in
\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G)
. Since the bottom row of (
3.7
) is exact, the image of
\eta
in
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))
is
\mathbf{trans}([\bar{Y}])
for some torsor
\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
under
\bar{G}
such that
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
. In view of Proposition
3.8
, there is a continuous homomorphism
i:E\to\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
fitting in a commutative diagram

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 151.4776pt\raise-26.8125pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{q}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 168.43576pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 168.43576pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Gamma_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 209.60593pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 209.60593pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

In particular,
i
is necessarily injective. Thus, we may regard
E
as a subgroup of
\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
,
i.e.
, the pair
(\bar{Y},E)
defines a descent type
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
, whose image in
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
is precisely
\eta
. This proves exactness of the top row of (
3.7
) at the term
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
.

Finally, it is immediate from our construction that (
3.7
) is functorial in
X
and
G
.

We conclude this § with the following arithmetic application, which shall be used in a subsequent work. Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective geometrically integral variety over a number field
k
. We recall that the Brauer–Grothendieck group of
X
is
\operatorname{Br}(X):=\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,\mathbb{G}_{m})
. Let
\operatorname{Br}_{1}(X):=\operatorname{Ker}(\operatorname{Br}(X)\to\operatorname{Br}(\bar{X}))
be its “algebraic” subgroup. Let
\Omega
denote the set of places of
k
, and let

\Be(X):=\operatorname{Ker}\left(\operatorname{Br}_{1}(X)\to\prod_{v\in\Omega}\frac{\operatorname{Br}_{1}(X_{k_{v}})}{\operatorname{Img}(\operatorname{Br}(k_{v})\to\operatorname{Br}(X_{k_{v}}))}\right)

be the subgroup of
\operatorname{Br}_{1}(X)
consisting of “everywhere locally constant” elements. We recall that
X(k_{\Omega}):=\prod_{v\in\Omega}X(k_{v})
. Let us define
X(k_{\Omega})^{\Be(X)}
to be the subset of
X(k_{\Omega})
consisting of families of local points which are orthogonal to
\Be(X)
relative to the Brauer–Manin pairing (see
e.g.

[
Sko01
, (5.2)]
, noting that the elements of
\Be(X)
are “unramified” thanks to
[
Har94
, Théorème 2.1.1]
).

Proposition 3.9
.

Keep the above notations and assumptions, and let
L=(\bar{G},\kappa)
be a
k
-kernel, where
\bar{G}
is a connected linear algebraic
\bar{k}
-group. If
X(k_{\Omega})^{\Be(X)}\neq\varnothing
, then every descent type
\lambda\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
comes from a torsor
Y\to X
under a
k
-form
G
of
L
.

Proof.

Let
\bar{G}^{\operatorname{u}}
be the unipotent radical of
\bar{G}
,
\bar{G}^{\operatorname{red}}:=\bar{G}/\bar{G}^{\operatorname{u}}
(which is reductive),
\bar{T}
the abelianization of
\bar{G}^{\operatorname{red}}
(it is the maximal toric quotient of
\bar{G}
), and
\pi:\bar{G}\to\bar{T}
the canonical projection. Since
\operatorname{Ker}(\pi)
is characteristic in
\bar{G}
, the outer action
\kappa
on
\bar{G}
induces an
action
of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{T}
, hence a
k
-form
T
of
\bar{T}
. By Construction
2.6
and Lemma
2.7
, we have a map
\pi_{\ast}:\operatorname{DType}(X,L)\to\operatorname{DType}(X,T)
. If
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,L)
, then
\pi_{\ast}\lambda=[\pi_{\ast}\bar{Y},\pi_{\ast}E]
, where
[\pi_{\ast}E]=\pi_{\ast}[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,T)
. By Theorem
3.7
, the natural map
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,T)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,T)
takes
\pi_{\ast}[E]
to
0
. Under the assumption
X(k_{\Omega})^{\Be(X)}\neq\varnothing
, Harari and Skorobogatov have shown that this map is injective (combine Proposition 8.1 and Corollary 8.17 in
[
HS13
]
). It follows that
\pi_{\ast}[E]=0
. On the other hand, since
X(k_{\Omega})\neq\varnothing
, the restriction of
[E]
to
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k_{v},L)
is neutral for all
v\in\Omega
by virtue of Proposition
2.4

(ii)
. By
[
Bor93
, Proposition 6.5]
, we conclude that the class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,L)
is neutral. Now, the existence of an
X
-torsor of descent type
\lambda
follows from Proposition
2.4

(i)
.

Remarks 3.10
.

Proposition
3.9
has been shown in the case where
L=\mathbf{lien}(G)
for some finite commutative algebraic
k
-group
G
in
[
HW22
, Lemma 3.7]
.

4
Comparing descent types and extended types

In this §, we fix a perfect field
k
and a smooth geometrically integral variety
p:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
. We define the
units-Picard complex

\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})
of
X
to be the cone of the canonical morphism

\mathbb{G}_{m,k}[1]\to(\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})[1]

in the bounded-below derived category
\mathcal{D}^{+}(k)
of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules. By
[
BvH09
, Lemma 2.3, Corollary 2.5]
, the truncated complex
\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}
is quasi-isomorphic to
[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]
, with
\bar{k}(X)^{\times}
in degree
0
. Accordingly,
\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})
is represented by the complex
[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]
concentrated in degrees
-1
and
0
, and its cohomology is given by

\mathscr{H}^{-1}(\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))=\operatorname{U}(\bar{X}):=\bar{k}[X]^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}

and

\mathscr{H}^{0}(\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))=\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X}).

We note that the abelian group
\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})
is finitely generated and free. This can be easily seen if
X
has a smooth compactification
X^{c}
, (that is, a smooth proper variety containing
X
as a dense open subset
2
2

2

such a compactification always exists when
k
has characteristic
0
, by resolution of singularities.
), since in this case we have an inclusion
\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Div}_{\infty}(\bar{X}^{c})
, where
\operatorname{Div}_{\infty}
means the group of Weil divisors supported outside
\bar{X}
. For a characteristic-free proof, see
[
Bri18
, Proposition 4.1.2]
.

Let
G
be a smooth
3
3

3

i.e.
, the characteristic of
k
does not divide the cardinality of the torsion subgroup of
\widehat{G}
.

k
-group of multiplicative type. When
Y\to X
is a torsor under
G
, its
type
(a notion due to Colliot-Thélène and Sansuc
[
CTS87
]
) is the Galois equivariant homomorphism

\mathbf{type}(Y):\widehat{G}\to\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X}),\quad\chi\mapsto\chi_{\ast}[\bar{Y}],

(we recall that
\widehat{G}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\bar{k}}(\bar{G},\mathbb{G}_{m})
,
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})
, and
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\mathbb{G}_{m})=\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X})
). Harari and Skorobogatov generalized the classical type into the
extended type

\mathbf{xtype}(Y)
, which is a morphism
\widehat{G}\to\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})
in the derived category
\mathcal{D}^{+}(k)
. More precisely, by
[
HS13
, Proposition 8.1]
, we have the top row of the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{xtype}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{trans}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G),}

(4.1)

the bottom row being issued from the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence

\mathrm{H}^{p}(k,\mathrm{H}^{q}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{p+q}(X,G).

Over
\bar{k}
, this tells us that
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))
, that is, an
\bar{X}
-torsor under
\bar{G}
is perfectly determined by its extended type. Over
k
, the extended type classifies torsors up to twisting by a Galois cocycle
\sigma\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,G)
. These features are similar to the those of the descent type. The aim of this § is to show that these two notions of types are in fact equivalent. We shall compare the top rows of (
3.7
) and (
4.1
).

If
\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})=0
(that is, if
\bar{k}[X]^{\times}=\bar{k}^{\times}
), then
G(\bar{X})=G(\bar{k})
and hence

\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))=\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X})).

In this case, the extended type coincides with the classical type, which gives an isomorphism

\mathbf{type}:\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X}))\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G})).

In other words, one may simply think of the type
\mathbf{type}(Y)
as the class
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
, just like in the classical descent theory of Colliot-Thélène–Sansuc. The other extreme case is when
\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X})=0
(for example, when
X
is a
k
-torsor under a torus). In this case, the classical type gives absolutely no information. In fact, the extended type is now an element of
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{U}(\bar{X}))
.

Here is the main result of this §.

Theorem 4.1
(Theorem
B
).

There exists a homomorphism

\Phi:\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))

of abelian groups, which is functorial in
X
and
G
. It fits in a diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{dtype}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\bar{Y},E]\mapsto[E]}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{xtype}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(X,G),}

(4.2)

which commutes up to a sign, where the bottom row is exact. If
X
is quasi-projective, then
\Phi
is an isomorphism.

4.1
Extended types revisited

The first step in the proof of Theorem
4.1
is to describe the map
\mathbf{xtype}
explicitly. Let
f:Y\to X
be a torsor under
G
. As in
[
HS13
, §2]
, we define
\bar{k}[Y]^{\times}_{G}\subseteq\bar{k}[Y]^{\times}
to be the
\Gamma_{k}
-submodule consisting of invertible functions
a
for which there exists a character
\chi\in\widehat{G}
such that
a(y\cdot g)=a(y)\chi(g)
for all
y\in Y(\bar{k})
and
g\in G(\bar{k})
, that is,
a\circ\rho_{g}=a\chi(g)
(where
\rho_{g}:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}
denotes the right action of
g
). This character is necessarily unique and shall be denoted by
\chi_{a}
. The homomorphism
\bar{k}[Y]^{\times}_{G}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto\chi_{a}}\widehat{G}
is
\Gamma_{k}
-equivariant. Indeed, for all
a\in\bar{k}[Y]^{\times}_{G}
,
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, and
g\in G(\bar{k})
, we have

\chi_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-5.735pt{s}\kern 2.18991pt{a}}}})(g),

where
s_{\#}
denotes the Galois action of
s
on
\bar{Y}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m,\bar{k}}
. It follows that
\chi_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-5.735pt{s}\kern 3.87215pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-5.735pt{s}\kern 4.99654pt{a}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-7.40451pt{s}\kern 4.99654pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-12.4576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.99654pt{a}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-4.81763pt{s}\kern 3.06589pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.42699pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.06589pt{a}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-3.94165pt{s}\kern 2.18991pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-7.55101pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.18991pt{a}}}}
as claimed.

We also define the subsheaf
(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}
of the étale sheaf
f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y}
on
X
as follows. For any étale morphism
U\to X
, the section group
(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}(U)\subseteq(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})(U)=\operatorname{Mor}_{U}(Y_{U},\mathbb{G}_{m,U})
consists of the
U
-morphisms
a:Y_{U}\to\mathbb{G}_{m,U}
for which there exists a morphism
\chi:G_{U}\to\mathbb{G}_{m,U}
of
U
-group schemes such that
a(y\cdot g)=a(y)\chi(g)
for all
y\in Y_{U}(\bar{k})
and
g\in G_{U}(\bar{k})
. We have an exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{G}_{m,X}\to(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}\to\widehat{G}_{X}\to 0

(4.3)

of étale sheaves on
X
(see
[
HS13
, Proposition 8.9(i)]
), generalizing the relative version of Rosenlicht’s lemma
[
CTS87
, Proposition 1.4.2]
. Note that
\bar{k}[Y]^{\times}_{G}=\bar{k}[Y]^{\times}
and
(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}=f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y}
in the case where
G
is a torus.

Under the identification
\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{G}_{m})
(
cf.

[
CTS87
, Proposition 1.4.1]
), the class
-[Y]
corresponds to the class of the extension (
4.3
) (see
[
HS13
, Proposition 8.9(ii)]
). Now, applying
\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}
to (
4.3
) yields a distinguished triangle

\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}\to\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}\to\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\widehat{G}_{X}\to\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}[1]

(4.4)

in
\mathcal{D}^{+}(k)
. Since
\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\widehat{G},-)=\mathbb{R}\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},-)\circ\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}
(see
e.g.

[
Wei94
, Corollary 10.8.3]
), we have a canonical isomorphism
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})
, which takes the class of (
4.3
) to that of the composite

\widehat{G}=p_{\ast}\widehat{G}_{X}\to\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\widehat{G}_{X}\to\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}[1],

the last arrow being the one from (
4.4
). Here, we have
\widehat{G}=p_{\ast}\widehat{G}_{X}
because
\bar{X}
is connected. As in the proof of
[
HS13
, Proposition 8.1]
, we have
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})
. The above morphism hence factors through a morphism
\widehat{G}\to(\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})[1]
, which, after composing with the canonical map
(\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})[1]\to\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})
, yields a morphism in
\mathcal{D}^{+}(k)
representing
-\mathbf{xtype}([Y])
. Our aim now is to describe the cone of
\widehat{G}\to(\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})[1]
as an explicit
2
-term complex.

Recall that
\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}
is quasi-isomorphic to the complex
[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]
concentrated in degrees
0
and
1
. We start by investigating
\bar{k}(X)^{\times}
. Let
K:=k(X)
and
L:=\bar{k}\otimes_{k}L=\bar{k}(X)
. The exact sequence (
4.3
) for the torsor
Y_{L}\to\operatorname{Spec}(L)
reads

1\to\bar{L}^{\times}\to\bar{L}[Y_{L}]^{\times}_{G_{L}}\to\widehat{G}\to 0.

Taking Galois cohomology (noting that
\mathrm{H}^{1}(L,\mathbb{G}_{m})=0
by Hilbert’s Theorem 90 and
\mathrm{H}^{0}(L,\widehat{G})=\widehat{G}
since
G
is split over
\bar{k}\subseteq L
), one obtain an exact sequence

1\to\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\to L[Y_{L}]^{\times}_{G_{L}}\to\widehat{G}\to 0.

(4.5)

On the other hand, taking cohomology of (
4.4
) yields an exact sequence

1\to\bar{k}[X]^{\times}\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}\bar{k}[Y]^{\times}_{G}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto\chi_{a}}\widehat{G}\to\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X}).

(4.6)

For each non-empty open subset
U\subseteq X
, it is clear that
\bar{k}[Y_{U}]^{\times}_{G}\subseteq L[Y_{L}]^{\times}_{G_{L}}
. We define the
\Gamma_{k}
-module

\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}:=\varinjlim_{U\subseteq X}\bar{k}[Y_{U}]^{\times}_{G},

(4.7)

which
a priori
is a subgroup of
L[Y_{L}]^{\times}_{G_{L}}
. Since
\widehat{G}
is finitely generated as an abelian group, the composite
\widehat{G}\to\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X})\to\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{U})
is
0
if
U
is a sufficiently small. As
\bar{k}(X)^{\times}=\varinjlim\limits_{U\subseteq X}\bar{k}[U]^{\times}
, we have an exact sequence

1\to\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto\chi_{a}}\widehat{G}\to 0.

(4.8)

Comparing (
4.5
) to (
4.8
), we see that, in fact,
\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}=L[Y_{L}]^{\times}_{G_{L}}
.

Let us now inspect the term
\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})
. Although the
k
-variety
Y
is not necessarily integral, it is smooth and pure-dimensional and we can talk about Weil divisors on it. Call an integral divisor
Z\subseteq Y

horizontal
if it is the Zariski closure of a divisor on the generic fibre
Y_{K}\to\operatorname{Spec}(K)
of
f
, and
vertical
otherwise (
i.e.
, if
f(Z)
does not contain the generic point of
X
). Let
\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{hor}}(Y)
(resp.
\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver}}(Y)
) denote the (free) abelian groups of horizontal (resp. vertical) divisors on
Y
. Then
\operatorname{Div}(Y)=\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{hor}}(Y)\oplus\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver}}(Y)
. We say that a divisor
D=\sum_{\eta\in Y^{(1)}}n_{\eta}[\eta]\in\operatorname{Div}(Y)
is
G
-invariant
if for every
\xi\in X^{(1)}
and
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in f^{-1}(\xi)
, one has
n_{\eta}=n_{\eta^{\prime}}
. If
G
is a torus, then every divisor is
G
-invariant because
f
has geometrically integral fibres. We write
\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},G}(Y)\subseteq\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver}}(Y)
for the subgroup of vertical,
G
-invariant divisors. Then the flat pullback
f^{\ast}:\operatorname{Div}(X)\to\operatorname{Div}(Y)
is injective with image
\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},G}(Y)
.

Over
\bar{k}
, a divisor
D\in\operatorname{Div}(\bar{Y})
is
\bar{G}
-invariant if and only if
\rho_{g}^{\ast}D=D
for all
g\in G(\bar{k})
(as always, we use
\rho_{g}
to denote the automorphism
y\mapsto y\cdot g
of
\bar{Y}
). Since
\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}=L[Y_{L}]^{\times}_{G_{L}}
, any function
a\in\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}
is regular on the generic fibre of
\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
,
i.e.
,
\operatorname{div}(a)\in\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver}}(\bar{Y})
. Furthermore, it follows from the definition of the groups
\bar{k}[Y_{U}]^{\times}_{G}
that
\operatorname{div}(a)\in\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})
. In view of (
4.8
), we have an exact sequence

0\to[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}[\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})]\to\widehat{G}\to 0

(4.9)

of complexes of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules, where the two complexes are concentrated in degrees
0
and
1
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a torsor under
G
. Under the isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m})=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]),

the element
[Y]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)
corresponds to the inverse class of the extension (
4.9
). Consequently, the extended type
\mathbf{xtype}([Y])\in\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}({\widehat{G}},[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})])
is the inverse class of the extension

0\to[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}[\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})]\to\widehat{G}\to 0.

(4.10)

Proof.

The exact sequence (
4.3
) for the generic fibre
Y_{K}\to\operatorname{Spec}(K)
is

0\to\mathbb{G}_{m,K}\to(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y_{K}})_{G_{K}}\to\widehat{G}_{K}\to 0.

Let
j:\operatorname{Spec}(K)\to X
denote the inclusion of the generic point. Since
\mathbb{R}^{1}j_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,K}=0
by Hilbert’s Theorem 90 and since
j_{\ast}\widehat{G}_{K}=j_{\ast}(p\circ j)^{\ast}\widehat{G}=j_{\ast}j^{\ast}p^{\ast}\widehat{G}=j_{\ast}j^{\ast}(\widehat{G}_{X})=\widehat{G}_{X}
, we have an exact sequence

0\to j_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,K}\to j_{\ast}(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y_{K}})_{G_{K}}\to\widehat{G}_{X}\to 0

(4.11)

of étale sheaves on
X
. Let
\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv_{X}
denote the sheaf of Weil divisors on
X
, and
\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv^{\operatorname{ver},G}_{Y}
the sheaf of vertical,
G
-invariant divisors on
Y
. Then, (
4.3
) and (
4.11
) fit in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{G}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{\ast}(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{G}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv^{\operatorname{ver},G}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0.}

Since the left column is exact, it is also the case for the middle one. In particular, we have quasi-isomorphisms

\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}\simeq\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}[j_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,K}\to\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv_{X}]

and

\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}\simeq\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}[j_{\ast}(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}\to\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv^{\operatorname{ver},G}_{Y}].

Now, any complex of sheaves
\mathcal{F}
on
X_{\textnormal{\'{e}t}}
yields a canonical morphism
p_{\ast}\mathcal{F}\to\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathcal{F}
in
\mathcal{D}^{+}(k)
, which is functorial in
\mathcal{F}
. Since
p_{\ast}j_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,K}=\bar{k}(X)^{\times}
,
p_{\ast}\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv_{X}=\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})
,
p_{\ast}j_{\ast}(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,Y})_{G}=\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}
, and
p_{\ast}\mathscr{D}\kern-0.5ptiv^{\operatorname{ver},G}_{Y}=\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})
, we obtain a morphism of distinguished triangles from (
4.9
) to (
4.4
),
i.e.
, a commutative diagram

\textstyle{[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}
\textstyle{[\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})][1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}(f_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X})_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\widehat{G}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}[1].}

Since the complex
[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]
represents the object
\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}
, it follows from the discussion at the beginning of this paragraph that
-\mathbf{type}([Y])
is precisely the class of the morphism
\widehat{G}\to[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})][1]
from the top row of the above diagram,
i.e.
, the class of (
4.9
).

4.2
The comparison map

Proposition
4.2
gives us a hint on how to construct an element of
\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))
from a descent type
\lambda=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
(instead of a torsor
Y\to X
under
G
). We have a complex
[\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})]
of
abelian groups
, and we shall use the subgroup
E\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
to define a suitable Galois action on it.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
f:\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
be a torsor under
\bar{G}
such that
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
. Recall that the sequence

1\to G(\bar{X})\to\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)\xrightarrow{q}\Gamma_{k}\to 1

is exact (cf. (
3.9
)). We define an action of
\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
on
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
by the formula

\forall\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X),\forall a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}},\quad\alpha\cdot a:=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ a\circ\alpha^{-1},

(4.12)

Then the following are true.

(i).

The action (
4.12
) is well defined, and the map
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto\chi_{a}}\widehat{G}
is
q
-equivariant.

(ii).

The group
G(\bar{X})=\operatorname{Ker}(q)
acts trivially on the image of the inclusion
f^{\ast}:\bar{k}[X]^{\times}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
, and the induced action of
\Gamma_{k}=\operatorname{Coker}(q)
on this image is the natural Galois action.

(iii).

Let
\phi:X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism between smooth geometrically integral
k
-varieties, and let
\bar{Y}^{\prime}:=\phi^{\ast}\bar{Y}=\bar{Y}\times_{\bar{X}}\bar{X}^{\prime}
. Recall from Construction
2.5
that we have a homomorphism
\phi^{\ast}:\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)\to\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}^{\prime}/X^{\prime})
. The pullback homomorphism
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}\to\bar{k}[\bar{Y}^{\prime}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
is
\phi^{\ast}
-equivariant.

(iv).

Let
\varphi:G\to H
be a morphism of groups of
k
-groups of multiplicative type,
\bar{Z}\to\bar{X}
a torsor under
H
such that
[\bar{Z}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{H}))
, and
\iota:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Z}
a
\varphi
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-morphism. Let
\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
, and let
\beta\in\operatorname{SAut}_{H}(\bar{Z}/X)
be a
q(\alpha)
-semilinear automorphism fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\bar{Z}.}

Then
\alpha\cdot\iota^{\ast}a=\iota^{\ast}(\beta\cdot a)
for all
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Z}]_{\bar{H}}^{\times}
.

Proof.

(i).

Let
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]_{\bar{G}}^{\ast}
,
\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
, and
s:=q(\alpha)
. For any
