4.99654pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-12.4576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.99654pt{a}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-4.81763pt{s}\kern 3.06589pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-8.42699pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.06589pt{a}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi^{{\kern-3.94165pt{s}\kern 2.18991pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-7.55101pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.18991pt{a}}}}
, which is what needed to be proved.

(ii).

Recall that the inclusion
G(\bar{X})=\operatorname{Mor}_{\bar{k}}(\bar{X},\bar{G})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X))
is given by
\sigma\mapsto\rho_{\sigma}^{-1}
, where
\rho_{\sigma}:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}
is the
\bar{X}
-automorphism
y\mapsto y\cdot\sigma(f(y))
. For
b\in\bar{k}[X]^{\times}
and
\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
, we have

\alpha\cdot(f^{\ast}b)=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ b\circ f\circ\alpha^{-1}=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ b\circ q(\alpha)^{-1}_{\#}\circ f=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-12.93663pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-33.41609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-12.93663pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-33.41609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.8632pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-23.49138pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.26237pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-22.89055pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}\circ f=f^{\ast}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-12.93663pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-33.41609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-12.93663pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 3.51666pt}}_{{\kern-33.41609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.51666pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.8632pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 2.1029pt}}_{{\kern-23.49138pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.1029pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{q(\alpha)}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}b^{{\kern-8.26237pt{q(\alpha)}\kern 1.50208pt}}_{{\kern-22.89055pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.36513pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.30885pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.64919pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.50208pt}}}),

which is what claimed.

(iii).

Let
\pi:\bar{Y}^{\prime}\to\bar{Y}
be the canonical projection. For each
\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
, we have
\alpha\circ\pi=\pi\circ\phi^{\ast}\alpha
by the construction of
\phi^{\ast}\alpha
. This implies

\phi^{\ast}\alpha\cdot\pi^{\ast}a=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ a\circ\pi\circ(\phi^{\ast}\alpha)^{-1}=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ a\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ\pi=(\alpha\cdot a)\circ\pi=\pi^{\ast}(\alpha\cdot a)

for all
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]_{\bar{G}}^{\ast}
. The claim follows.

(iv).

We have
\alpha\cdot\iota^{\ast}a=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ a\circ\iota\circ\alpha^{-1}=q(\alpha)_{\#}\circ a\circ\beta^{-1}\circ\iota=(\beta\cdot a)\circ\iota=\iota^{\ast}(\beta\cdot a)
.

Construction 4.4
.

Let
f:\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}
be a torsor under
\bar{G}
such that
[\bar{Y}]\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(k,\mathrm{H}^{1}(\bar{X},\bar{G}))
, and let
E\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
be a subgroup extension of
\Gamma_{k}
by
G(\bar{k})
. Then we have an exact sequence

1\to\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto\chi_{a}}\widehat{G}\to 0

of abelian groups (
cf.
(
4.8
)). Equipped with the action (
4.12
), the above sequence is an exact sequence of
E
-module (Lemma
4.3

(i)
,
(ii)
, and
(iii)
). For
a\in\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
and
g\in G(\bar{k})
, we have
\rho^{-1}_{g}\cdot a=a\circ\rho_{g}=a\chi_{a}(g)
, hence
[\rho_{g}^{-1}\cdot a]=[a]
in
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
. In otherwords,
G(\bar{k})
acts trivially on the quotient
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
. It follows that we have an exact sequence

1\to\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto\chi_{a}}\widehat{G}\to 0

of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules. By Lemma
4.3

(iv)
, the isomorphism class of the
\Gamma_{k}
-module
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
only depends on the descent type
[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
, and not the pair
(\bar{Y},E)
representing it.

Lemma 4.5
.

With the above notations, the following are true.

(i).

If
[\bar{Y},E]=\mathbf{dtype}(Y)
for some torsor
Y\to X
under
G
, then the above action of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}/\bar{k}^{\times}
is the natural Galois action.

(ii).

In general,
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
is a discrete
\Gamma_{k}
-module.

Proof.

(i).

Let
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and lift it to the semilinear automorphism
s_{\#}:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}
. Then
s_{\#}\cdot a=s_{\#}\circ a\circ s_{\#}^{-1}=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-6.74562pt{s}\kern 4.33765pt}}_{{\kern-11.7987pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.33765pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-6.74562pt{s}\kern 4.33765pt}}_{{\kern-11.7987pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.33765pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-4.34183pt{s}\kern 2.59009pt}}_{{\kern-7.95119pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.59009pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}a^{{\kern-3.60179pt{s}\kern 1.85005pt}}_{{\kern-7.21115pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.85005pt}}}
for all
a\in\bar{k}(Y)^{\times}_{G}
. The claim follows.

(ii).

Take any non-empty affine open subset
U\subseteq X
. Then
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}=\bar{k}(\bar{Y}_{\bar{U}})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
, and thus we may assume that
X
is affine. Let
K/k
be a finite extension trivializing the class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
. By Proposition
2.4

(i)
, we have the restriction of
[\bar{Y},E]
to
K
is
\mathbf{dtype}(Y)
for some torsor
Y\to X_{K}
under
G_{K}
. It follows from
(i)
that the stabilizer of any function
a\in\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
is an open subgroup of
\Gamma_{K}
, hence also an open subgroup of
\Gamma_{k}
.

Now, we define a Galois action on
\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})
using the isomorphism
f^{\ast}:\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})
. Then the map
\operatorname{div}:\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\to\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})
is
\Gamma_{k}
-equivariant. Indeed, let
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and lift it to an
s
-semilinear automorphism
\alpha\in E
. Let
a\in\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
and write
\operatorname{div}(a)=f^{\ast}D
for some (unique)
D\in\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})
. Since
f\circ\alpha^{-1}=s_{\#}^{-1}\circ f
, we have

\operatorname{div}(\alpha\cdot a)=(\alpha^{-1})^{\ast}\operatorname{div}(a)=(\alpha^{-1})^{\ast}f^{\ast}D=f^{\ast}(s_{\#}^{-1})^{\ast}D=f^{\ast}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}D^{{\kern-9.16174pt{s}\kern 6.75377pt}}_{{\kern-14.21483pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.75377pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}D^{{\kern-9.16174pt{s}\kern 6.75377pt}}_{{\kern-14.21483pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.75377pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}D^{{\kern-5.94464pt{s}\kern 4.1929pt}}_{{\kern-9.554pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.1929pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}D^{{\kern-4.74666pt{s}\kern 2.99492pt}}_{{\kern-8.35602pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.99492pt}}}).

To sum up, we have an exact sequence

0\to[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]\xrightarrow{f^{\ast}}[\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})]\to\widehat{G}\to 0.

(4.13)

of complexes of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules, similar to (
4.10
). Let
\lambda:=[\bar{Y},E]\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
. We define

\Phi(\lambda)\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})])=\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))

to be the inverse class of the above extension. We obtain a map

\Phi:\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})).

By virtue of Lemma
4.3

(iii)
and
(iv)
, the map
-\Phi
functorial in
X
and
G
.

Lemma 4.6
.

The map
\Phi:\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))
from Construction
4.4
is a homomorphism.

Proof.

Let
\lambda_{1}=[\bar{Y}_{1},E_{1}]
and
\lambda_{2}=[\bar{Y}_{2},E_{2}]
be descent types on
X
bound by
\mathbf{lien}(G)
. We consider their external product
\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2}=[\bar{Y},E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2}]\in\operatorname{DType}(X\times_{k}X,G\times_{k}G)
, where
\bar{Y}:=\bar{Y}_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{Y}_{2}
(
cf.
Definition
3.1
). Recall that the sum
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}\in\operatorname{DType}(X,G)
is defined to be
\nabla_{\ast}\Delta^{\ast}(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})
, where
\nabla:G\times_{k}G\to G
and
\Delta:X\to X\times_{k}X
are the multiplication and diagonal morphisms, respectively (see Definition
3.4
).

For
i=1,2
, denote by
f_{i}:\bar{Y}_{i}\to\bar{X}
the structure morphism, and by
\pi_{i}:\bar{Y}\to\bar{Y}_{i}
and
p_{i}:\bar{X}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X}\to\bar{X}
the projections. Let
\varsigma=p_{1}^{\ast}+p_{2}^{\ast}:\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})\oplus\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})\to\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X})
and
\delta=\Delta^{\ast}:\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X})\to\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})
, then
\delta\circ\varsigma
is the sum map on
\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})
. On the other hand, the Cartier dual
\widehat{\nabla}:\widehat{G}\to\widehat{G}\oplus\widehat{G}
of
\nabla
is the diagonal map. It follows from the definition of the addition on
\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))
(the “Baer sum”) that

-\Phi(\lambda_{1})-\Phi(\lambda_{2})=\widehat{\nabla}^{\ast}\delta_{\ast}\varsigma_{\ast}((-\Phi(\lambda_{1}))\oplus(-\Phi(\lambda_{2}))).

Since the map
\Phi
is functorial in
X
and
G
, we have

-\Phi(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})=-\Phi(\nabla_{\ast}\Delta^{\ast}(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2}))=\widehat{\nabla}^{\ast}(-\Phi(\Delta^{\ast}(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})))=\widehat{\nabla}^{\ast}\delta_{\ast}(-\Phi(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})).

Thus, it would be enough to prove that

\varsigma_{\ast}((-\Phi(\lambda_{1}))\oplus(-\Phi(\lambda_{2})))=-\Phi(\lambda_{1}\times\lambda_{2})\in\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X})).

To this end, we are to establish a commutative diagram

\textstyle{[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]^{\oplus 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}^{\ast}\oplus f_{2}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{2}[\bar{k}(\bar{Y}_{i})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}}(\bar{Y}_{i})]^{\oplus 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}^{\ast}+\pi_{2}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\widehat{G}\oplus\widehat{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\bar{k}(X\times_{k}X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}f_{2})^{\ast}}
\textstyle{[\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}^{\operatorname{ver},\bar{G}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{G}}(\bar{Y})]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{G\times_{k}G}}

of complexes of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules. The left square commutes because we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{\bar{Y}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\bar{X}}
\textstyle{\bar{X}\times_{\bar{k}}\bar{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{\bar{X}.}

To see that the right square also commutes, take
a_{i}\in\bar{k}(\bar{Y}_{i})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
for
i=1,2
, and let
\chi_{i}:=\chi_{a_{i}}\in\widehat{G}
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{((\pi_{1}^{\ast}a_{1})(\pi_{2}^{\ast}a_{2}))\circ\rho_{(g_{1},g_{2})}}{(\pi_{1}^{\ast}a_{1})(\pi_{2}^{\ast}a_{2})}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{a_{1}\circ\pi_{1}\circ\rho_{(g_{1},g_{2})}}{a_{1}\circ\pi_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{a_{2}\circ\pi_{2}\circ\rho_{(g_{1},g_{2})}}{a_{2}\circ\pi_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{a_{1}\circ\rho_{g_{1}}\circ\pi_{1}}{a_{1}\circ\pi_{1}}\right)\left(\frac{a_{2}\circ\rho_{g_{2}}\circ\pi_{2}}{a_{2}\circ\pi_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}(g_{1})\chi_{2}(g_{2})

for all
g_{1},g_{2}\in G(\bar{k})
. This means
\chi_{(\pi_{1}^{\ast}a_{1})(\pi_{2}^{\ast}a_{2})}=(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})
under the identification
\widehat{G\times_{k}G}=\widehat{G}\oplus\widehat{G}
.

It remains to check that the map
\pi_{1}^{\ast}+\pi_{2}^{\ast}
is a morphism of complexes of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules
. On the level of vertical invariant divisors, this is obvious thanks to the definition of the Galois action on the divisor groups. On the level of rational functions, take
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and
a_{i}\in\bar{k}(\bar{Y}_{i})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
, and let
\alpha_{i}\in E_{i}
be a lifting of
s
for
i=1,2
. The element
\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}\in E_{1}\boxtimes E_{2}
is a lifting of
s
, and we have

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2})\cdot((\pi_{1}^{\ast}a_{1})(\pi_{2}^{\ast}a_{2}))

\displaystyle=s_{\#}\circ((\pi_{1}^{\ast}a_{1})(\pi_{2}^{\ast}a_{2}))\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(s_{\#}\circ(\pi_{1}^{\ast}a_{1})\circ(\alpha_{1}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}^{-1}))(s_{\#}\circ(\pi_{2}^{\ast}a_{2})\circ(\alpha_{1}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=(s_{\#}\circ a_{1}\circ\pi_{1}\circ(\alpha_{1}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}^{-1}))(s_{\#}\circ a_{2}\circ\pi_{2}\circ(\alpha_{1}^{-1}\times_{\bar{k}}\alpha_{2}^{-1}))

\displaystyle=(s_{\#}\circ a_{1}\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}\circ\pi_{1})(s_{\#}\circ a_{2}\circ\alpha_{2}^{-1}\circ\pi_{2})

\displaystyle=((\alpha_{1}\cdot a_{1})\circ\pi_{1})((\alpha_{2}\cdot a_{2})\circ\pi_{2})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{1}^{\ast}(\alpha_{1}\cdot a_{1}))(\pi_{2}^{\ast}(\alpha_{2}\cdot a_{2}))

in
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
, or
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{}^{{\kern-2.40797pt{s}\kern 0.0pt}}_{{\kern-7.46106pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 0.0pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{}^{{\kern-2.40797pt{s}\kern 0.0pt}}_{{\kern-7.46106pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 0.0pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{}^{{\kern-1.75174pt{s}\kern in
\bar{k}(\bar{Y})^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
, which is what needed to be proved.

4.3
Comparing two fundamental exact sequences

We prove Theorem
4.1
in this paragraph. By Proposition
4.2
and Lemma
4.5

(i)
, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{dtype}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{1}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{xtype}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))}

commutes. Once the commutative diagram (
4.2
) is established, its top row is exact if
X
is quasi-projective, by Theorem
3.7
. In this case, it follows that
\Phi
is an isomorphism by the five lemma.

Thus, it remains to establish commutativity up to a sign of the diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{DType}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G).}

Let
\lambda:=[\bar{Y},E]
be a descent type on
X
bound by
\mathbf{lien}(G)
. We proceed to show that
\partial(\Phi(\lambda))=-[E]
. The first reduction step is a localization. By functoriality, we may replace
X
by any non-empty open subset (this does not affect the class
[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
,
cf.
Construction
2.5
). Since
\mathscr{H}^{-1}(\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))=\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})
and
\mathscr{H}^{0}(\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))=\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X})
, we have a distinguished triangle

\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})[1]\to\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})\to\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X})\to\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})[2]

in the category
\mathcal{D}^{+}(X)
. Applying
\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},-)
to it yields an exact sequence

0\to\operatorname{Ext}_{k}^{1}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{U}(\bar{X}))\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X}))

(4.14)

Let us consider the image
\lambda^{\prime}
of
\Phi(\lambda)
in
\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X}))
. Since
\widehat{G}
is finitely generated, for some sufficiently small non-empty open subset
U\subseteq X
, the composite
\widehat{G}\xrightarrow{\lambda^{\prime}}\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{X})\to\operatorname{Pic}(\bar{U})
is
0
. Replacing
X
by
U
, we may and shall from now on assume that
\lambda^{\prime}=0
. This means we have a short exact sequence

0\to\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})\to\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\to\widehat{G}\to 0

(4.15)

by applying
\mathscr{H}^{0}
to (
4.13
). Let
\varepsilon\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{U}(\bar{X}))
denote its class. It is clear that we have a morphism from (
4.15
) to (
4.13
), hence the map
\operatorname{Ext}_{k}^{1}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{U}(\bar{X}))\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))
from (
4.14
) takes
\varepsilon
to
-\Phi(\lambda)
.

Since
\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})
is a finitely generated free abelian group (see the beginning of this §), the group
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
is also finitely generated. Let
T
(resp.
M
) be the
k
-torus (resp.
k
-group of multiplicative type) with character module
\widehat{T}=\operatorname{U}(\bar{X})
(resp.
\widehat{M}=\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
). We have a commutative diagram with solid arrows

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}[X]^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\widehat{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma_{2}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]_{\bar{G}}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma_{1}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{\widehat{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}

(4.16)

with exact rows and columns, in the category of
abelian groups
. Its top row and right column are exact sequences of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules. Let
\tau\in\mathrm{Z}^{1}(k,T)
be a cocycle such that
-[\tau]\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,T)
corresponds to the class of the top row, under the identification
\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,T)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{T},\mathbb{G}_{m})

[
CTS87
, Proposition 1.4.1]
.

To describe
\partial(\Phi(\lambda))
, we recall how the map
\partial:\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
was defined. According to the proof of
[
HS13
, Proposition 8.1]
, the bottom row of (
4.2
) is obtained by applying
\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},-)
to the triangle

\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{R}p_{\ast}\mathbb{G}_{m,X}\to\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X})[-1]\to\mathbb{G}_{m}[1],

or equivalently, the extension of complexes

0\to\bar{k}^{\times}\to[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]\to[\bar{k}(X)^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{div}}\operatorname{Div}(\bar{X})]\to 0.

(4.17)

Thus, under identification
\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{k}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{G}_{m})

[
CTS87
, Proposition 1.4.1]
, the map
\partial
is given by the Yoneda product with the class of (
4.17
). There is a commutative diagram of Yoneda pairings

\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{G},\widehat{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{T},\mathbb{G}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\rule{0.90417pt}{0.90417pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{k}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{G}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\widehat{G},\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}))}
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{k}(\operatorname{UPic}(\bar{X}),\mathbb{G}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\rule{0.90417pt}{0.90417pt}}}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{k}(\widehat{G},\mathbb{G}_{m})}

Viewing that there is an obvious inclusion from the top row of (
4.16
) to (
4.17
), the above diagram gives us
\partial(\Phi(\lambda))=\varepsilon\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\rule{1.29167pt}{1.29167pt}}}}[\tau]
. Alternatively, we have
\partial(\Phi(\lambda))=\delta([\tau])
, where
\delta:\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,T)\to\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
is the connecting homomorphism issued from the exact sequence of
k
-groups of multiplicative type

1\to G\to M\to T\to 1

(4.18)

dual to the right column of (
4.16
). The task now is to show that
\delta([\tau])=-[E]
.

Let us now take the dotted arrows in diagram (
4.16
) into consideration. Since
\bar{k}^{\times}
is a divisible abelian group, the bottom row has a section
\varsigma_{1}:\widehat{M}\to\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
. By exactness of the middle column, the composite
\widehat{T}\to\widehat{M}\xrightarrow{\varsigma_{1}}\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
factors through a section
\varsigma_{2}:\widehat{T}\to\bar{k}[X]^{\times}
(in the category of abelian groups). Now, the section
\varsigma_{1}
(resp.
\varsigma_{2}
) yields a homomorphism
\pi^{\ast}:\bar{k}[M]\to\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]
(resp.
\varpi^{\ast}:\bar{k}[T]\to\bar{k}[X]
) of
\bar{k}
-algebras (see
cf.
paragraph
1.2
). Since
\bar{M}
and
\bar{T}
are affine, we obtain
\bar{k}
-morphisms
\pi:\bar{Y}\to\bar{M}
and
\varpi:\bar{X}\to\bar{T}
fitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bar{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\bar{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varpi}
\textstyle{\bar{T},}

(4.19)

where both vertical arrows are torsors under
\bar{G}
, and
\pi
is
\bar{G}
-equivariant.

One should keep in mind that
\pi
induces a homomorphism (also denoted by)

\pi^{\ast}:\bar{k}[M]^{\times}_{M}\to\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}

of abelian groups, which fits in a commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}[M]^{\times}_{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b\mapsto\chi_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\widehat{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a\mapsto[a]}
\textstyle{\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(4.20)

(the top row is exact because it is (
4.6
) applied to the trivial torsor
M\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)
). By the five lemma,
\pi^{\ast}
is an isomorphism, which induces identity on the Galois module
\widehat{M}
. Considering the action
\cdot
of
\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
introduced in (
4.12
), we have
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-11.27342pt{s}\kern 8.86545pt}}_{{\kern-16.3265pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.86545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-11.27342pt{s}\kern 8.86545pt}}_{{\kern-16.3265pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.86545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-7.06406pt{s}\kern 5.31232pt}}_{{\kern-10.67342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.31232pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-5.54622pt{s}\kern 3.79448pt}}_{{\kern-9.15558pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.79448pt}}}=[\alpha\cdot a]\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}=\widehat{M}
for any
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, any lifting
\alpha\in E
of
s
, and any
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
. Conversely, we shall need the following Lemma, whose proof is postponed to the end of this §.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
s\in\Gamma_{k}
and let
\alpha\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
be a lifting of
s
. Then
\alpha\in E
if and only if
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-11.27342pt{s}\kern 8.86545pt}}_{{\kern-16.3265pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.86545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-11.27342pt{s}\kern 8.86545pt}}_{{\kern-16.3265pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.86545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-7.06406pt{s}\kern 5.31232pt}}_{{\kern-10.67342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.31232pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-5.54622pt{s}\kern 3.79448pt}}_{{\kern-9.15558pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.79448pt}}}=[\alpha\cdot a]
in
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}=\widehat{M}
for all
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
.

End of proof of Theorem
4.1
, modulo Lemma
4.7
.

Let
Z
be the
k
-torsor under
T
defined by the cocycle
\tau
. This means
\bar{Z}=\bar{T}
as a
\bar{k}
-variety, and the action
\cdot_{\tau}
of
\Gamma_{k}
on
\bar{Z}
is given by
s\mapsto\rho_{\tau_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}
(where
s_{\#}:T\to T
is the usual Galois action). By
[
CTS87
, Propositions 1.4.1, 1.4.2 and 1.4.3]
, the class in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{k}(\widehat{T},\mathbb{G}_{m})\cong\mathrm{H}^{1}(k,T)
of the extension

1\to\bar{k}^{\times}\to\bar{k}[Z]^{\times}\to\widehat{T}\to 0,

is
-[\tau]
, that is, the class of the top row of (
4.16
). This means
\varpi^{\ast}:\bar{k}[Z]\to\bar{k}[X]
is in fact a homomorphism of
k
-algebra (because it induces an isomorphism
\bar{k}[Z]^{\times}\to\bar{k}[X]^{\times}
of
\Gamma_{k}
-modules thanks to the five lemma, and
\bar{k}[Z]^{\times}
generates the
\bar{k}
-vector space
\bar{k}[Z]^{\times}
). In other words,
\varpi
descends into a
k
-morphism
X\to Z
.

Recall that our objective is to show the identity
\delta([\tau])=-[E]\in\mathrm{H}^{2}(k,G)
. If
\mu:\Gamma_{k}\to M(\bar{k})
is a cochain lifting
\tau
, then
\delta([\tau])
is the class of the
2
-cocycle

g:\Gamma_{k}\times\Gamma_{k}\to G(\bar{k}),\quad g_{s,t}:=\mu_{s}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mu^{{\kern-7.2703pt{s}\kern (4.21)

For each
s\in\Gamma_{k}
, let
\beta_{s}:=\rho_{\mu_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}
. Since
\mu_{s}
is a lifting of
\tau_{s}
, one sees that
\beta_{s}\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{M}/Z)
is
s
-semilinear. From diagram (
4.19
), we obtain a morphism
\psi=(f,\pi):\bar{Y}\to\bar{X}\times_{\bar{Z}}\bar{M}
of
\bar{X}
-torsors under
\bar{G}
, which, like any morphism of torsors, is an isomorphism. Let
\operatorname{pr}:\bar{X}\times_{\bar{Z}}\bar{M}\to\bar{M}
denote the second projection, then
\operatorname{pr}\circ\psi=\pi
. Let

\alpha_{s}:=\psi^{-1}\circ(s_{\#}\times_{\bar{Z}}\beta_{s})\circ\psi\in\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X),

which is
s
-semilinear. For each
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
, write it as
a=\pi^{\ast}b=b\circ\pi
for a unique
b\in\bar{k}[M]^{\times}_{M}
and let
\chi_{b}=[a]\in\widehat{M}=\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
be the character of
M
associated with
b
(using the notations from diagram (
4.20
)). We have

\displaystyle\alpha_{s}\cdot a

\displaystyle=s_{\#}\circ b\circ\pi\circ\psi^{-1}\circ(s_{\#}\times_{\bar{Z}}\beta_{s})^{-1}\circ\psi,

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{eq:ActionOfSAut}},

\displaystyle=s_{\#}\circ b\circ\operatorname{pr}\circ(s_{\#}\times_{\bar{Z}}\beta_{s})^{-1}\circ\psi,

\displaystyle\text{since }\operatorname{pr}\circ\psi=\pi,

\displaystyle=s_{\#}\circ b\circ\beta_{s}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{pr}\circ\psi,

\displaystyle\text{since }\beta_{s}\circ\operatorname{pr}=\operatorname{pr}\circ(s_{\#}\times_{\bar{Z}}\beta_{s}),

\displaystyle=s_{\#}\circ b\circ s_{\#}^{-1}\circ\rho_{\mu_{s}}\circ\pi,

\displaystyle\text{since }\operatorname{pr}\circ\psi=\pi\text{ and }\beta_{s}=\rho_{\mu_{s}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#},

5.31232pt}}_{{\kern-10.67342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.79448pt}}_{{\kern-9.15558pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.79448pt}}}
in
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}=\widehat{M}
. By Lemma
4.7
, we have
\alpha_{s}\in E
. To conclude, we have constructed a continuous set-theoretic section
\alpha:\Gamma_{k}\to E
. Finally, for
s,t\in\Gamma_{k}
, one has

\beta_{st}\circ\beta_{t}^{-1}\circ\beta_{s}^{-1}=\rho_{\mu_{st}}^{-1}\circ(st)_{\#}\circ t_{\#}^{-1}\circ\rho_{\mu_{t}}\circ s_{\#}^{-1}\circ\rho_{\mu_{s}}=\rho_{\mu_{st}}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}\circ t_{\#}\circ t_{\#}^{-1}\circ s_{\#}^{-1}\circ\rho_{\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mu^{{\kern-6.11174pt{s}\kern 4.24889pt\mathchoice{\makebox[2.81831pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.81831pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.26389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.90277pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\kern-9.7211pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.24889pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mu^{{\kern-6.11174pt{s}\kern 4.24889pt{t}}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.35072pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.17188pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mu^{{\kern-3.50302pt{s}\kern 1.50636pt{t}}}}}\circ\rho_{\mu_{s}}=\rho_{g_{s,t}}

using (
4.21
). It follows that
\alpha_{st}\circ\alpha_{t}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{s}^{-1}=\rho_{g_{s,t}}=\rho_{g_{s,t}^{-1}}^{-1}
since
\psi
is a
\bar{G}
-equivariant
\bar{X}
-isomorphism. From this, we conclude that
[E]=-[g]=-\delta([\tau])
, which achieves the proof.

To complete the proof of Theorem
4.1
, it remains to prove Lemma
4.7
.

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

Let
E^{\prime}\subseteq\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)
be the subgroup consisting of
G
-semiequivariant automorphisms
\alpha
for which
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-11.27342pt{s}\kern 8.86545pt}}_{{\kern-16.3265pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.86545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-11.27342pt{s}\kern 8.86545pt}}_{{\kern-16.3265pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 8.86545pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-7.06406pt{s}\kern 5.31232pt}}_{{\kern-10.67342pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.31232pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{s}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}[a]^{{\kern-5.54622pt{s}\kern 3.79448pt}}_{{\kern-9.15558pt\mathchoice{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.77432pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29686pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.79448pt}}}=[\alpha\cdot a]
in
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
for all
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
, where
s\in\Gamma_{k}
denotes the image of
\alpha
. We have seen that
E\subseteq E^{\prime}
. In order to show that
E=E^{\prime}
, it suffices to check that the sequence
1\to G(\bar{k})\to E^{\prime}\to\Gamma_{k}\to 1
is exact. Of course, only exactness at the middle term
E^{\prime}
needs verification. Let
\alpha\in E^{\prime}
whose image in
\Gamma_{k}
is
1
. Since the sequence
1\to G(\bar{X})\to\operatorname{SAut}_{G}(\bar{Y}/X)\to\Gamma_{k}\to 1
is exact,
\alpha
has the form
\alpha(y)=y\cdot\sigma(f(y))^{-1}
for some
\bar{k}
-morphism
\sigma:\bar{X}\to\bar{G}
. By definition,
[\alpha\cdot a]=[a]
in
\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}/\bar{k}^{\times}
for all
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
. Hence, we have a homomorphism

c:\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}\to\bar{k}^{\times},\quad c(a):=\frac{\alpha\cdot a}{a}.

Let
x\in X(\bar{k})
and lift it to a point
y\in\bar{Y}(\bar{k})
. For any
a\in\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}
, one has

c(a)=\frac{(\alpha\cdot a)(y)}{a(y)}=\frac{a(\alpha^{-1}(y))}{a(y)}=\frac{a(y\cdot\sigma(x))}{a(y)}=\chi_{a}(\sigma(x)).

We have assumed that the map last arrow in (
4.14
) takes
\Phi(\lambda)
to
0
, hence we have an exact sequence

1\to\bar{k}[X]^{\times}\to\bar{k}[\bar{Y}]^{\times}_{\bar{G}}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto\chi_{a}}\widehat{G}\to 0

of abelian groups. By the above calculation,
c
factors through a homomorphism
\bar{c}:\widehat{G}\to\bar{k}^{\times}
defined by
\bar{c}(\chi)=\chi(\sigma(x))
for
any

x\in X(\bar{k})
. Since the pairing
G(\bar{k})\times\widehat{G}\to\bar{k}^{\times}
is perfect, there exists a unique
g\in G(\bar{k})
such that
\chi(g)=\chi(\sigma(x))
for all
x\in X(\bar{k})
and
\chi\in\widehat{G}
. Also by perfectness, this implies that
\sigma
is the constant
\bar{k}
-morphism
x\mapsto g
. In other words,
\alpha=\rho_{g}^{-1}
, the element of
\operatorname{Aut}_{\bar{G}}(\bar{Y}/\bar{X})
corresponding to
g\in G(\bar{k})
. The lemma is proved.
